For example, after Switch fails for target A, a first Switch to target B reads `operation in failedProfileOperations` and emits `is_retry=true`; after an Add failure, dismissing and reopening Add leaves the Create enum in the set, so a new create session is also marked retry. No device reproduction is claimed because an operation failure was not induced.
- **Expected:** Retry attribution follows the same logical target/dialog session, is cleared when that session is cancelled or succeeds, and never adds profile names/IDs to event parameters.
- **Actual:** Retry is keyed only by operation type; target and session changes are ignored, and cancellation does not clear the set entry.
- **Frequency:** After any failed operation, the next operation of the same type in that ViewModel is marked as a retry, regardless of target or session.
- **Evidence:** [QA-BUG-0057 retry-attribution source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-27/qa-bug-0057-profile-retry-attribution-source-audit.txt); `feature/home/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/home/ui/appsettings/AppSettingsViewModel.kt` (`failedProfileOperations` and `runProfileOperation`).
- **Root cause:** Retry history is stored by operation enum rather than by a bounded in-memory logical attempt/session key.
- **Affected files:** App Settings ViewModel/operation tests and associated QA evidence/results.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `c0d8545d` (`fix(settings): scope profile retry attribution [QA-BUG-0057]`). Retry history now uses private in-memory per-target keys for switches and unique per-dialog session keys for create/rename/delete. New sessions and cancellation clear stale markers; success clears only the matching key. No retry key or profile identifier is added to event parameters.
- **Retest:** Host `:feature:home:ui:testAndroidHostTest` PASS, including matching-target retry, different-target first attempt, new-dialog-session first attempt, cancellation cleanup, same-session failure-to-retry success attribution, and success clearing without affecting another key. `:androidApp:assembleDebug` PASS. Installed on Samsung over existing data with no uninstall/data clear; local and remote APK SHA-256 `f410e98f799d359b4353af45f86fa12c61dd952a439dc112fd6e615aa742d6df`, package 0.4.5 (21), PID `20327`, source `c0d8545d` plus separate uncommitted QA-BUG-0049 candidate. App Settings route and Add dialog Cancel smoke passed; one local visible-route event, one route breadcrumb, one Add dialog-open/cancel pair, zero profile-operation attempt/mutation events, and zero exception/fatal markers. This is not a failed-write retry verification. The latter remains BLOCKED until a disposable profile and safe controlled-failure fixture exist. Full evidence: [QA-BUG-0057 retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-27/qa-bug-0057-profile-retry-attribution-retest.txt). DebugAnalyticsManager logs are local-only; Firebase Analytics delivery is waived and Crashlytics Console delivery remains unverified.

## QA-BUG-0058 — Rapid profile-submit tail tap reaches the underlying App Settings control

- **Severity / user impact:** Medium; a rapid repeat confirmation tap can toggle an unrelated setting after a fast profile operation dismisses its dialog, silently changing user preferences.
- **Status:** FIXED IN SOURCE (`63306140`); Samsung rapid-Add retest PASS for the reproduced two-tap sequence. Profile and setting cleanup verified. Rename/Delete confirmation variants remain NOT RUN; broader case 743 is not signed off.
- **Screen/test IDs:** App Settings/Profile case 743; new rapid-dismiss interaction case 764.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung serial `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; Parrot 0.4.5 (21), PID `7916`, APK SHA-256 `4b72393d8e0eabc532317c0f9ecc28f4a09e19ba868ca1d644ed43d53d641820`; source `63306140`, `7669f849`, `c0d8545d`, plus separate uncommitted QA-BUG-0049 changes.
- **Preconditions:** One existing profile is active; App Settings is visible; Add Profile dialog has a valid synthetic disposable name and the keyboard is hidden.
- **Exact reproduction (Samsung):** Send two taps to the Add confirmation center (`769,1381` on the 1080×2340 display) with a 1 ms interval. One Create operation and its automatic Switch each emitted exactly one attempted/succeeded pair; local timestamps were 21:11:08.797–21:11:08.805. The dialog/route closed before the second tap was consumed. The second tap reached the underlying Show Continue Reading toggle, changing it from enabled to disabled. The existing profile remained registered; no duplicate Create event/profile was observed. The setting was immediately restored to enabled, the disposable profile was switched away from and deleted, and the original profile remained active with exactly one profile afterward. No private profile label or ID, screenshot or raw hierarchy was retained.
- **Expected:** Taps belonging to the same rapid confirmation burst are either rejected by the in-flight guard or consumed by the modal surface; they must not activate a control exposed underneath the dismissed dialog.
- **Actual:** The operation guard prevented a duplicate profile write, but after the dialog disappeared a later tap in the burst toggled an unrelated preference.
- **Frequency:** Observed once in one two-tap run; intermittent timing depends on operation latency and tap cadence. One run does not establish a population rate.
- **Evidence:** [Sanitized Samsung rapid-submit and cleanup evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-27/qa-bug-0058-profile-submit-fallthrough.txt).
- **Root cause:** The local profile mutation completed and removed its modal surface before the rapid input sequence drained; a subsequent pointer event was dispatched to the newly exposed Settings content.
- **Affected files:** App Settings profile dialog/screen interaction and ViewModel operation lifecycle; regression tests and QA ledgers.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `63306140` (`fix(settings): shield taps after profile operations [QA-BUG-0058]`). A shared profile-operation shield consumes pointer input over the Home root during the accepted mutation and for a bounded 250 ms after it completes, preventing a queued tap from reaching newly exposed content. It clears in a `NonCancellable` cleanup path; host coverage verifies the repeat-tap window.
- **Retest:** PASS for the Samsung Add/two-tap reproduction on package 0.4.5 (21), PID `7916`, local and installed APK SHA-256 `4b72393d8e0eabc532317c0f9ecc28f4a09e19ba868ca1d644ed43d53d641820`. One Create attempt/success and one automatic Switch attempt/success; the second 1 ms tap did not toggle Show Continue Reading. Both Show Continue Reading and Open Last Book remained enabled. The synthetic profile was switched away from and deleted; one original profile remained active. Host `:feature:home:ui:testAndroidHostTest` and `:androidApp:assembleDebug` PASS; zero exception/fatal markers. The app did not emit a preference-change event because no preference changed; QA-BUG-0059 remains separately open. DebugAnalyticsManager events are local only; Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived, and Crashlytics Console delivery remains unverified. Rename/Delete rapid-confirm variants remain NOT RUN. Full evidence: [QA-BUG-0058 post-fix retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-27/qa-bug-0058-profile-submit-tail-shield-retest.txt).

## QA-BUG-0059 — Show Continue Reading preference changes are absent from Analytics

- **Severity / user impact:** Low for functionality, medium for product observability; preference adoption/change counts cannot be measured and an unexpected setting mutation is invisible in Analytics.
- **Status:** FIXED IN SOURCE (`9503b45e`); host schema/sanitizer test PASS and Samsung off/on Analytics retest PASS. Firebase ingestion is waived, and the shelf/bubble visual variant remains N-A because no current-reading fixture is available.
- **Screen/test IDs:** App Settings case 425; feature adoption/instrumentation case 749.
- **Device/build/commit:** Pre-fix observation: Samsung serial `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; Parrot 0.4.5 (21), PID `26130`, APK SHA-256 `136454932c2a4a7a02e2e7a1fd1afa0ded9de9159f0b76a64ca8a5a80aeaf12e`. Post-fix retest: package 0.4.5 (21), PID `14481`, APK SHA-256 `319fba7efbd020c8b799ecb82f5af249e99b89119b3a4749ffe939ec626b1a7c`, source commit `9503b45e` plus QA-BUG-0058 `63306140`, QA-BUG-0056 `7669f849`, QA-BUG-0057 `c0d8545d` and separate QA-BUG-0049 working-tree changes.
- **Preconditions:** User toggles Show Continue Reading in App Settings.
- **Exact reproduction / audit:** Source `AppSettingsViewModel.setShowContinueReading` writes the preference and updates state but does not call `analytics.logEvent`; no corresponding typed event exists under `lib/analytics/api`. During the QA-BUG-0058 two-tap reproduction the setting changed from enabled to disabled, yet the PID-scoped event-name trace contained no Show Continue Reading change event. The preference was restored to enabled after observation.
- **Expected:** Each committed preference change emits one bounded typed Analytics event with the resulting enabled state; no personal data is included. Ordinary preference persistence does not require a Crashlytics report.
- **Actual:** State/persistence changes without an Analytics event or operation breadcrumb.
- **Frequency:** Every Show Continue Reading toggle.
- **Evidence:** [QA-BUG-0058 Samsung trace](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-27/qa-bug-0058-profile-submit-fallthrough.txt); `AppSettingsViewModel.setShowContinueReading`; absence of an event definition/emission under `lib/analytics/api` and App Settings.
- **Root cause:** The preference handler updates storage/state but omits feature-usage instrumentation.
- **Affected files:** `feature/home/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/home/ui/appsettings/AppSettingsViewModel.kt`, `lib/analytics/api` App Settings event schema, sanitizer/event tests, and QA results.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `9503b45e` (`fix(analytics): track continue-reading toggle [QA-BUG-0059]`). Adds typed `AppSettingsAnalyticsEvent.ShowContinueReadingToggled`, event name `show_continue_reading_toggled`, and exactly one emission after the synchronous `ShowContinueReading` preference write. The payload contains only the allow-listed boolean `is_enabled`; sanitizer tests reject extra profile identifiers.
- **Retest:** PASS: `:feature:home:ui:testAndroidHostTest`, `:lib:analytics:implementation:testAndroidHostTest`, and `:androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeded. On the physical Samsung, direct off/on produced exactly one local event for each state (`false`, then `true`); the final preference and Open Last Book remained enabled, and no private identifiers were emitted. Zero exception/fatal markers. The Books screen had no shelf or bubble even when the setting was enabled before the test, so that visual variant is N-A (no current-reading fixture). Evidence: [Samsung toggle retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-27/qa-bug-0059-continue-reading-toggle-retest.txt). Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived; DebugAnalyticsManager output is local-only. Crashlytics Console delivery remains unverified.

## QA-BUG-0060 — Duplicate profile names produce indistinguishable profile tiles

- **Severity / user impact:** Medium; users may select the wrong profile and open or modify another profile's private library/settings when duplicate labels cannot be distinguished.
- **Status:** FIXED AND SAMSUNG-RETESTED (`bf38383e`). Add and Rename reject exact and case/whitespace-normalized duplicate names; legacy duplicate tiles are visibly distinguished with stable ordinals. Two disposable duplicate profiles remain temporarily for the immediately following long-name case, then must be removed and the original profile restored active.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Profiles case 415; related switching case 409.
- **Device/build/commit:** Physical Samsung serial `RFCWC0SSVDM`, model SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; Parrot 0.4.5 (21), debug, PID `16401`, APK SHA-256 `75ae77c5b811ba23991fbe893ed26632897475260310fa87f742e14d82b3d1a0`; source fix commit `bf38383e48f798c8d66e662798b3e88f01e57b62`. Build also contains separate uncommitted server-management and playback work; only the focused QA-BUG-0060 files were staged in its commit.
- **Preconditions:** At least one profile exists. Create two additional disposable profiles using the same synthetic display name.
- **Exact reproduction (Samsung, pre-fix):** In App Settings, create a disposable profile with a synthetic label; return to Settings, repeat Add Profile with the exact same label, and inspect the profile row. Both creations succeeded. The two cards showed the same truncated display label and identical avatar treatment; only the active card's check/green background differed. Tapping each card by position changed the active marker, but there was no visible per-profile distinction to identify which duplicate's data would be opened. No raw screenshot or profile label is retained. [Pre-fix evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-27/qa-bug-0060-duplicate-profile-name-reproduction.txt).
- **Expected:** Duplicate names are either rejected with safe validation or represented with enough stable, non-sensitive visual distinction that users can reliably choose the intended profile. This follows case 415's “allowed or rejected consistently — no confusion in switching” criterion.
- **Actual:** Pre-fix duplicates were accepted and visually ambiguous. On the fixed build, existing collisions receive distinct stable ordinal labels; new Add/Rename collisions are rejected before persistence and leave the form available for correction/cancellation.
- **Frequency:** Confirmed once with a pair of synthetic profiles; broader recurrence is deterministic if duplicate labels are allowed.
- **Evidence:** [Samsung pre-fix reproduction](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-27/qa-bug-0060-duplicate-profile-name-reproduction.txt) and [committed fix/retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-27/qa-bug-0060-duplicate-profile-name-fix-retest.txt); transient screenshots were inspected but not saved. Sanitized local event/breadcrumb assertions are in the retest record.
- **Root cause:** Source audit confirms that `AppSettingsViewModel.addProfile` and `renameProfile` submit any nonblank user string, `UserRegistryImpl.createProfile` keys only by random profile ID and performs no name-conflict check, and `ProfileItem` renders `profile.name` on one ellipsized line with the same avatar treatment for every inactive profile.
- **Affected files:** Profile create/rename validation in `AppSettingsViewModel`, App Settings view state/dialogs/resources, typed Analytics schema/sanitizer tests, validation helpers/tests, and QA catalogue/results. Normal UI Add/Rename must reject normalized collisions before persistence.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `bf38383e48f798c8d66e662798b3e88f01e57b62` (`fix(settings): disambiguate duplicate profiles [QA-BUG-0060]`). Adds normalized collision validation to Add/Rename, bounded validation Analytics and diagnostics without profile values/IDs, and deterministic `Profile 1` / `Profile 2` labels for any legacy normalized duplicate group.
- **Retest:** PASS on Samsung 2026-09-27, PID `16401`, hash-matched APK `75ae77c5…d1a0`. Exact and case/leading-trailing-whitespace collisions were independently rejected for Add and Rename. Four rejected submissions emitted exactly four local validation events and four matching breadcrumbs (two per operation); no profile-operation mutation attempt/success was emitted. Existing duplicate tiles showed stable distinct ordinals; switching either card updated the active marker and returned to the tab root. Host UI tests (20 total) and Analytics tests (33 total) passed with zero failures/errors/skips; debug APK assembled successfully. Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived, local logs are not Firebase evidence; no unexpected failure was induced and Crashlytics delivery is not asserted. Disposable-profile cleanup/original-profile restoration and case-416 long-name coverage remain pending and are recorded separately before proceeding.

## QA-BUG-0061 — Book-list sort and view-mode Analytics report type names instead of selected values

- **Severity / user impact:** Low for direct functionality, medium for product observability; sort and layout adoption/configuration cannot be segmented, making feature-usage and per-profile preference analysis unreliable.
- **Status:** FIXED AND SAMSUNG-RETESTED; focused commit `c8fbbd96`. Case 419's executed filter/sort/view-mode isolation variants now pass.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Profile isolation case 419 (Books filter/sort/layout); shared Analytics/provider schema checks.
- **Device/build/commit:** Physical Samsung serial `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21). Pre-fix: PID `16401`, APK SHA-256 `75ae77c5b811ba23991fbe893ed26632897475260310fa87f742e14d82b3d1a0`. Post-fix: PID `3552`, APK SHA-256 `17e8fe08e5c0209ccc5af2eb39357b9b4c378364ac7cc25d741b0a63d83a29b8`, repository HEAD `c66281ff` plus the uncommitted fix at installation; local and installed hashes match. Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived; DebugAnalyticsManager is local-only.
- **Preconditions:** Books screen open in a disposable profile; change a sort and its direction, and change list/grid layout.
- **Exact reproduction (Samsung):** In profile A, select Favorites, choose Date Added, toggle direction to Newest and tap Grid. PID `16401` local event trace contained `quick_filter_toggled(filter=FAVORITES,is_enabled=true)` at 00:08:50.407; `sort_changed(sort_config=BookSortConfig)` twice at 00:10:51.415 and 00:11:25.141; and, after a direct Grid tap at 00:21:27.142, `view_mode_changed(view_mode=BookListViewMode)`. The two sort events correspond to option selection and direction toggle. No profile labels/IDs or book data were used in the trace.
- **Expected:** Each sort selection emits a bounded value identifying the selected option and direction (for example `date_added_descending`); each layout change emits `list` or `grid`. Values are finite enum-derived categories and contain no user data.
- **Actual:** `BooksListViewModel` passes `sortConfig::class.simpleName` and `viewMode::class.simpleName`, so every event reports the constant type names `BookSortConfig` / `BookListViewMode`, not the selected values.
- **Frequency:** Every sort configuration and view-mode change, including changes within a different profile.
- **Evidence:** [Samsung pre-fix trace and state comparison](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0061-profile-settings-analytics-prefx.txt) and [post-fix Samsung retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0061-profile-settings-analytics-retest.txt); `feature/books/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/books/ui/list/BooksListViewModel.kt`.
- **Root cause:** Kotlin `::class.simpleName` was used where stable enum-derived field values were required; provider validation accepted these generic strings.
- **Affected files:** Books list ViewModel and tests; Analytics parameter sanitizer and regression tests; case-419 evidence/results and run report.
- **Fix reference / commit:** Commit `c8fbbd961927a3231e64314399597e8c2157602b` (`fix(analytics): report selected book list settings [QA-BUG-0061, test 419]`). Implements enum-derived values (`date_added_descending`, `title_descending`, `grid`/`list`) and a provider allowlist that rejects the old class names and unknown values.
- **Retest:** PASS on Samsung after `adb install -r`, with existing profile data retained. Host suites passed: 2 Books UI tests and 34 Analytics implementation tests; Android debug APK assembled and local/device SHA-256 matched. Local events reported the selected ascending/descending sort, `grid`/`list`, and `IN_PROGRESS` filter values; no old class-name values appeared. Seven manual profile switches each emitted one `is_retry=false` attempt/success pair and matching start/terminal breadcrumbs. Zero fatal/handled-exception/non-fatal markers; normal setting changes require no Crashlytics report. Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived and Crashlytics delivery is unverified. Case 419 passes for preference isolation; empty libraries mean actual content ordering/card rendering remain untested. See [post-fix evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0061-profile-settings-analytics-retest.txt).

## QA-BUG-0062 — Reader setting save failures are presented and measured as successes

- **Severity / user impact:** Medium; a Reader setting can appear applied while its database write failed, then revert after reopening. Users receive no failure/retry feedback, and feature telemetry overstates committed changes.
- **Status:** FIXED — host-injected failure/retry regression and Samsung normal-save/Undo retest passed. A real Samsung database failure was not induced; the device failure variant remains blocked, not passed.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Reader Settings 185–231, extension cases 625–638; profile isolation case 420.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), debug; fix candidate built from worktree base `ef8eaf59ddc78ba2a4639eb9e225b83ac3d53d76` plus the QA-BUG-0062 patch. APK SHA-256 `8ac931ab87b487f88951d39968cdce53c7df193c624b4b6f1f6d4b282279f3d1`; installed and pulled APK hashes matched. Device PID `29954` during retest.
- **Preconditions:** Reader Settings visible; a setting is changed; local database save returns `Err`.
- **Exact reproduction / audit:** Source trace in [pre-fix evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0062-reader-settings-save-source-audit.txt): `SettingsViewModel.updateReaderSetting` logs `setting_changed`, immediately updates UI/Undo state, then launches `saveReaderSettingsUseCase` and discards its `CompletableResult`. On a returned failure, no ViewModel failure, rollback or retry path runs. A later reopen may show the previous stored value. Device-side induced failure is NOT RUN because a safe database fault fixture is unavailable.
- **Expected:** Emit an attempt when accepted and success only after persistence; on failure restore the last persisted state, show retryable feedback, emit failure/retry outcomes, and attach a bounded operation breadcrumb. Preserve coroutine cancellation and do not Crashlytics-report ordinary cancellation/Undo/picker dismissal.
- **Actual:** A pre-save `setting_changed` event and optimistic value/Undo feedback occur regardless of whether the save returns success or failure; the result is ignored.
- **Frequency:** Any failed reader-setting write, including theme, typography, layout, progress, navigation or ReadAloud preferences.
- **Evidence:** [Pre-fix source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0062-reader-settings-save-source-audit.txt); [save/Undo retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0062-reader-settings-save-retest.txt); `SettingsViewModel.kt`, `ReaderDataRepository.kt`, `ReaderLocalDataSource.kt` and `DatabaseExecutorImpl.kt`.
- **Root cause:** The UI boundary treats asynchronous persistence as fire-and-forget and emits success-like usage telemetry before observing the result.
- **Affected files:** Settings UI ViewModel/state/screen and Analytics event schema/provider allowlist; Reader data repository/database reporting boundary; targeted tests; catalogue/results/run report.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `1a1c747aeec5f3ce1c310c592a8956ffd5e8e60f` (`fix(settings): handle reader preference save outcomes [QA-BUG-0062]`). The focused commit includes the persistence/recovery implementation, regression tests, event schema, and pre/post-fix evidence; unrelated worktree changes were not staged.
- **Retest:** PASS for the failure/retry and Undo ViewModel regressions on host tests; PASS on Samsung for normal save, Undo persistence, local attempt/success events and correlated start/terminal breadcrumbs. The combined Settings/Home/Analytics host tests and debug APK assembly passed. No real database failure was induced on Samsung; case 768's device failure/retry variant is BLOCKED pending a safe fixture. Case 420 profile-isolation comparison remains NOT RUN. Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived; Crashlytics delivery is unverified.

## QA-BUG-0063 — Reader Settings may accept changes before the active profile's saved settings load

- **Severity / user impact:** Medium if reproduced; a fast first edit after opening Settings could be built from defaults and overwrite other stored Reader settings.
- **Status:** SUSPECTED by source audit only; reproduction NOT RUN. No change has been made for this item.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Reader Settings initialization; new extension case 769; related profile isolation case 420 and restart/persistence case 231.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21); source commit `c8fbbd961927a3231e64314399597e8c2157602b` plus separate uncommitted changes. Source audit only; no device setting was changed for this item.
- **Preconditions:** Open Reader Settings for a profile with non-default saved preferences; trigger a setting change before the initial settings Flow emits.
- **Exact reproduction / audit:** `SettingsViewState` starts with `isLoading=false` and default `ReaderSettingsUiModel`; `SettingsViewModel.observeReaderSettings` hydrates asynchronously without changing readiness; `SettingsScreen` ignores `isLoading` and renders the controls. The target model is constructed from the visible state. No delayed-emission or device race has been reproduced.
- **Expected:** Do not accept writes until stored settings for the active profile have been observed and controls reflect them.
- **Actual by source audit:** No loading/ready gate prevents an early change from saving a defaults-derived full settings object. Actual occurrence/frequency remain unknown.
- **Evidence:** [Source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0063-reader-settings-initial-load-source-audit.txt); `SettingsViewState.kt`, `SettingsViewModel.observeReaderSettings`, and `SettingsScreen.kt`.
- **Root cause:** Initial UI state is actionable before the asynchronous settings flow establishes the active profile's persisted value.
- **Affected files:** Settings view state/ViewModel/screen and focused delayed-flow tests if confirmed.
- **Fix reference / commit:** None; awaiting reproduction.
- **Retest:** NOT RUN. Requires a deterministic delayed-initial-emission fixture; do not call fixed unless a controlled test demonstrates the guard.

## QA-BUG-0064 — Statistics database read failures are rendered as genuine zero/empty data

- **Severity / user impact:** Medium; if any local aggregation query fails, users can be told there is no reading activity and see zero totals/streaks despite saved history. Statistics become misleading or unavailable, though reading data is not modified.
- **Status:** FIXED — deterministic host fault-injection/regression and successful retry passed; Samsung normal-empty-state/refresh regression passed. Samsung database fault injection was not performed, so direct device failure delivery remains unverified.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Statistics dashboard aggregate 385–386, 405–406, 421; failure/recovery extension 708. Detail query failures are tracked independently as QA-BUG-0066.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), debug, PID `29919`, APK SHA-256 `8b85ed4fbe5fa29d4a8c345f85bb9e1b649b5acc293a3943ffaea3822e29d9a2`; source fix commit `e09d76fe` (full hash in run report); installed by update with app data preserved.
- **Preconditions:** at least one statistics DAO/local-source read fails while the profile contains reading history; no such fixture/failure was available on the Samsung.
- **Exact reproduction / audit:** pre-fix, inject an `Err(DatabaseError(...))` for an aggregate query in `StatisticsLocalSource`, then call `GetReadingStatisticsUseCase`. `StatisticsDataRepository.getReadingStatistics` converted numeric errors to `0L`, list errors to `emptyList()`, map errors to `emptyMap()`, then emitted `Ok` with defaults; `calculateStreak` also defaulted a failed reading-day query to an empty list. The ViewModel therefore treated the result as success, and the Screen displayed its new-profile empty hint when session/book counts were zero. Post-fix, the host fake injects aggregate and reading-day `Err` values and verifies propagation; the ViewModel fake injects `DatabaseError`, verifies an actionable retry state and one contextual report, then retries successfully.
- **Expected:** query error remains an error, never a false zero/empty success; show bounded failure feedback and a usable retry/refresh path; emit attempt and terminal outcome analytics plus useful, correlated diagnostic context without profile/book payloads.
- **Actual:** dashboard query failures now propagate to the ViewModel, which keeps the last successful values when refreshing or presents a retryable failure state when no values exist. Attempt/success/failure outcomes and correlated operation breadcrumbs are added. Samsung verified the real-empty state and refresh; the database-error branch itself was verified with host fault injection, not on the phone. Detail-sheet failure behavior is a separate bug.
- **Frequency:** unknown; runtime failure was not induced.
- **Evidence:** [Case 421 fixture assessment and source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0064-statistics-failure-source-audit.txt); `feature/statistics/data/.../StatisticsDataRepository.kt`, `feature/statistics/ui/.../StatisticsViewModel.kt`, and `StatisticsScreen.kt`.
- **Root cause:** pre-fix aggregation used local-source `getOrElse` fallback values for query errors instead of propagating `AppError`; the UI used the same zero values for a real empty profile.
- **Affected files:** Statistics aggregation repository, dashboard ViewModel/view state/screen, typed load Analytics event schema and sanitizer, focused regression tests, and QA evidence/results.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `e09d76fe` — `fix(statistics): surface aggregation failures and retry [QA-BUG-0064]`.
- **Retest:** PASS for 3 data fault-injection tests, 1 ViewModel failure/retry test, 26 Analytics sanitizer tests, debug APK build, Samsung fresh-profile empty-state rendering and pull-to-refresh. Samsung query-failure injection is NOT RUN/BLOCKED because no safe device database fixture exists. Local debug logs confirmed initial-load and refresh attempt/success events and matching start/terminal breadcrumbs. Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived; Crashlytics backend delivery is unverified. [Final device/build/host retest evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0064-statistics-dashboard-retest.txt).

## QA-BUG-0065 — Statistics screen exposures lack source attribution and route breadcrumbs

- **Severity / user impact:** Low; feature adoption cannot reliably distinguish bottom-tab entry from the App Settings row, and a route sequence is missing from production diagnostic breadcrumbs.
- **Status:** FIXED — visible-route event attribution and breadcrumbs verified on Samsung for the App Settings row and Books bottom tab; four host attribution variants and parameter sanitization passed.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Statistics 385–408, 421, 427, 505, 695, 709; navigation/observability cases 740–749.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), debug, PID `2528`, APK SHA-256 `f08af30ff2015b574e9cc944d5bf116aece2c75db8f3724428168f8cf7a5c6ae`; focused code commit `466967f9` (full hash in run report). The installed APK was built from the same QA-BUG-0065 source tree immediately before its focused commit and update-installed without clearing data.
- **Preconditions:** enter Statistics from the bottom tab and from App Settings.
- **Exact reproduction / audit:** Pre-fix, `StatisticsViewModel.init` logged the parameterless `StatisticsAnalyticsEvent.StatisticsViewed`; the Home navigation visible-route observer had no Statistics branch. Post-fix, it attributes the visible route using bounded prior-destination/tab context and emits a matching route-visible breadcrumb. On Samsung, App Settings → Reading statistics emitted one `statistics_viewed` with `source_screen=app_settings, entry_point=statistics_row` and one `statistics_route` visible breadcrumb. Books → Statistics emitted one with `source_screen=books, entry_point=bottom_navigation` and one matching breadcrumb.
- **Expected:** once per visible Statistics destination, emit a bounded screen exposure with source screen and entry point, plus a matching route-visible diagnostic breadcrumb; avoid emission on recomposition.
- **Actual:** verified entry paths carry bounded source/entry-point parameters and a matching visible breadcrumb. Host-only tests also cover route restore and Back attribution; those two paths have not yet been executed on the Samsung.
- **Frequency:** pre-fix omission was deterministic; after the fix one event and one breadcrumb were observed per tested route transition, with no duplicate in the local PID logs.
- **Evidence:** [Case 421 fixture assessment and source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0064-statistics-failure-source-audit.txt); `StatisticsViewModel.kt`, `StatisticsAnalyticsEvent` in `BookAnalyticsEvent.kt`, and visible-route observer in `HomeNavigation.kt`.
- **Root cause:** Statistics view tracking is emitted from ViewModel construction rather than the navigation owner after the route becomes visible; the route is omitted from exposure instrumentation.
- **Affected files:** Statistics event schema/ViewModel, Home navigation exposure observer, sanitizer and context tests, and QA evidence/results.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `466967f9` — `fix(statistics): attribute visible route exposures [QA-BUG-0065, test 770]`.
- **Retest:** PASS for 4 host exposure-context tests, the Statistics load failure/retry regression (1), Analytics parameter sanitizer suite (26), and debug APK build. Samsung PASS for App Settings-row and Books bottom-tab entry (case 770 partial): one bounded `statistics_viewed` plus matching `statistics_route` visible breadcrumb for each. Statistics rendered after each route; current-PID fatal/handled-exception markers absent. Samsung Series-tab, route restore, Back return, and rotation/recomposition duplicate subvariants remain NOT RUN. Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived; local logs prove app-side event construction only. Crashlytics delivery was not tested. [Samsung and host retest evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0065-statistics-exposure-retest.txt).

## QA-BUG-0066 — Statistics detail query failures silently dismiss their sheets

- **Severity / user impact:** Low; a failed local read removes the requested detail sheet without an explanation or recovery action. Statistics detail is unavailable until the user tries again.
- **Status:** FIXED — deterministic host fault/retry tests pass for period-books, Books Read and Total Sessions; Samsung normal empty-detail flows and operation telemetry pass. Samsung detail-query failure injection remains BLOCKED; runtime frequency is unknown.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Statistics detail actions 387–395, 398–399; failure/recovery and telemetry extensions 708–709.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), debug, PID `15709`, APK SHA-256 `3d01dec69e98f5f2bcfef76faced86aee9284015e552b3499f6b324c0bbc675b`; focused fix commit `4f49c80c3d014211a1752b2af72c2ce23a724e2f`. APK was built from the same QA-BUG-0066 source tree before commit, update-installed without clearing Parrot data, and pulled back from the device with a matching hash. The build tree also contained separately uncommitted Home playback, Reader playback and Server Management work, excluded from this fix commit.
- **Preconditions:** a period, Books Read, or Total Sessions detail query returns `Err(DatabaseError(...))`.
- **Pre-fix exact reproduction / audit:** tap a period card, Books Read, or Total Sessions with the corresponding use case returning an error. In the pre-fix `StatisticsViewModel.loadBooksForPeriod`, `showBooksRead`, and `showRecentSessions` handlers, `error.log(...)` was followed by clearing the corresponding detail state. The bottom sheet was removed without user-facing error/retry feedback.
- **Expected:** retain a clear, retryable detail error state; emit one attempt and terminal outcome (including cancellation where user-triggered) and correlated, bounded operation breadcrumbs; report an unexpected failure at this UI boundary once, without private book/session data.
- **Actual before fix:** detail state was nulled, so the requested sheet disappeared; generic string-context exception logging existed, but no attempt/success/failure event or operation breadcrumb was present.
- **Frequency:** unknown; no runtime fault was injected.
- **Evidence:** [QA-BUG-0066 source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0066-statistics-detail-failure-source-audit.txt); `feature/statistics/ui/.../StatisticsViewModel.kt` and the period/books/sessions detail sheets.
- **Root cause:** pre-fix detail-query failure handlers discarded the detail state rather than representing an error/retry state; detail operation telemetry/context was absent.
- **Affected files:** Statistics detail view state/ViewModel and sheets, typed Analytics events and sanitizer, regression tests, and QA evidence/results.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `4f49c80c3d014211a1752b2af72c2ce23a724e2f` — `fix(statistics): keep detail failures retryable [QA-BUG-0066, test 771]`.
- **Retest:** PASS for 4 Statistics host tests: dashboard recovery regression; injected query failure plus retry for all three detail types; dismissal cancellation; and system-Back cancellation/callback. PASS for 26 Analytics parameter, 12 common diagnostic-payload and 1 Android diagnostic-payload sanitizer tests. `--rerun-tasks` full debug assembly succeeded. Host faults retained the requested sheet/error state, retries succeeded, each failure produced one bounded failure event, correlated breadcrumb pair and one contextual exception call; dismiss/Back produced one cancellation and no exception. Samsung PASS for normal period, Books Read and sessions empty-state sheets and dismissal; local output showed one attempt and one terminal success per query (Today was opened twice during settling, yielding two distinct attempt/success pairs), each with matching correlation IDs. Samsung fault injection/retry UI remains BLOCKED because no safe local DB failure fixture exists. Local debug Analytics output is not Firebase delivery evidence; Analytics ingestion is waived. Crashlytics backend delivery is unverified. [Samsung and host retest evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0066-statistics-detail-retest.txt).

## QA-BUG-0067 — Startup/database lifecycle logs may expose profile identity

- **Severity / user impact:** Low direct functional impact; medium privacy/diagnostic risk because startup logs can persist locally or be included in shared diagnostics.
- **Status:** Fixed in source commit `9789f5a1f13846e680e50aa03682d03e284d884c`; host checks and Samsung normal-startup/database-switch log checks PASS. The no-active-profile startup recovery branch remains NOT RUN and is tracked as an incomplete case-772 variant.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Startup recovery extension 772; related privacy audit case 509 and profile create/switch/delete cases 409–413.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), debug; post-redaction installed APK SHA-256 `75e418871ec005041a2f01259c080662bf4009d76aca7bef3763fd34f8916fd3`, source includes fix commit `9789f5a1` and QA-BUG-0055 working-tree changes at build time. PID `11251` was used for startup and delete-lifecycle checks. No profile label/ID was copied into evidence.
- **Preconditions:** Startup recovery runs when profiles exist but no active profile is selected; normal startup also logs the active profile ID.
- **Exact reproduction / audit:** Inspect `DefaultUserInitializer.ensureDefaultUserExists`: recovery messages interpolate `firstProfile.name` and `firstProfile.id`; the created-default branch interpolates `defaultProfile.id`; the normal active-profile branch interpolates `getActiveProfileId()`. Also inspect `DatabaseManager.switchToUserLocked`: active-profile changes log `currentUserId` and `userId`, and database-open logs interpolate `userId`. These templates can put profile values in Kermit logs during startup/recovery and database switching.
- **Expected:** Startup/recovery diagnostic messages use bounded operation-only text and never include profile names, IDs/UUIDs, credentials or user-authored values.
- **Actual before fix:** Source contained log templates interpolating profile name/ID. **After fix:** initializer and database lifecycle messages are stable operation-only strings. Samsung local output showed `Active profile already set`, `Active profile changed; switching database` and `Opened database for active profile`; no profile identifiers were found in those app messages. The no-active-profile startup recovery branch did not occur in this run.
- **Frequency:** Each startup path that activates an existing profile, creates the default profile, or reports an already active profile; each database profile switch/open.
- **Evidence:** [Source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0067-initializer-profile-log-source-audit.txt) and [host/Samsung redaction retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0067-profile-log-retest.txt). Only bounded messages and aggregate scanner results are retained; no raw log or profile value is stored.
- **Root cause:** Initializer and database-manager log templates interpolate profile-domain values instead of stable operation-only messages.
- **Affected files:** `DefaultUserInitializer.kt`, `DatabaseManager.kt`, and focused source/log-sanitization checks.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `9789f5a1f13846e680e50aa03682d03e284d884c` — `fix(privacy): redact profile IDs from lifecycle logs [QA-BUG-0067, test 772]`.
- **Retest:** PARTIAL PASS. Host source-message checks passed (1 initializer test, 1 database-manager test) and Android debug assembly passed; Samsung observed safe initializer/database switch messages on a valid-active-profile startup. PID-scoped UUID-like values were limited to diagnostic `correlation_id` fields and platform WorkManager/Kotzilla tags; no profile identity was retained. A startup with an actually missing/invalid active-profile selection is NOT RUN because no safe fixture exists. Analytics and Crashlytics delivery are N-A for this local logging privacy invariant.

## QA-BUG-0068 — Share Logs can report success and fail silently

- **Severity / user impact:** Medium; a user can be told or measured as having shared diagnostics when Android never opened the chooser, the log file disappeared, or file access failed. The user receives no recovery guidance.
- **Status:** FIXED in the current source tree; host fault-path regressions and Samsung normal/empty-log retests PASS. Injected read/chooser failures remain BLOCKED on Samsung; chooser dismissal/recipient selection is not observable through the current platform API.
- **Screen/test IDs:** App Settings cases 432–433; extensions 721–722 and 725/share; observability case 749.
- **Device/build/commit:** Pre-fix audit ran on Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), PID `11251`, APK SHA-256 `75e418871ec005041a2f01259c080662bf4009d76aca7bef3763fd34f8916fd3`. Fixed retest: same device/package/version, PID `30017`, APK SHA-256 `c1c120b592b8a66ff86cd01a742fcd369fc63fac97678e53880969aacc07fcf1`; tested source tree is committed as `9acf3381be57bc96f64e05ad5d610dcc71d7f3f6`. The APK was built before the commit from the identical fix tree, alongside unrelated pre-existing changes. No raw log contents or profile data were retained.
- **Preconditions:** The user invokes Share Logs, with either a nonempty, empty, or unreadable log file; Android file-provider/chooser startup may fail.
- **Exact reproduction / audit:** Pre-fix source trace: `AppSettingsViewModel.shareLogs()` read `getLogContents()`, treated an empty string as no logs, emitted `LogsShared`, then called `fileSharer.shareFile`. `AndroidFileLogger.getLogContents()` mapped read exceptions to empty content. `AndroidFileSharer.shareFile()` returned silently for a missing file and caught exceptions from FileProvider/`startActivity`; the common interface returned no outcome. Thus failures did not reach the ViewModel and `LogsShared` was emitted before chooser invocation. The event could not establish recipient acceptance or delivery.
- **Expected:** Distinguish empty logs from read/share failure; emit attempt and terminal results at the operation boundary; show retryable feedback and one contextual diagnostic for unexpected failures. Do not claim recipient selection or delivery. Never include log contents, paths or identifiers in telemetry.
- **Actual (pre-fix):** Read, missing-file and chooser-launch failures were suppressed, could appear empty/no-op, and `logs_shared` was emitted before invoking the platform action. **Fixed:** platform read/share failures now propagate to the ViewModel; only an empty file uses the ordinary no-logs message. New bounded events distinguish attempt, unavailable, launch success, failure and coroutine cancellation. Launch success means the platform accepted the chooser request, not that a recipient selected or received the file. Failure UI offers Retry and the error boundary reports one contextual non-fatal.
- **Frequency:** Deterministic for any handled share error or missing-file return; normal chooser cancellation/delivery outcome is not observable through the current `FileSharer` contract.
- **Evidence:** [Sanitized source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/app-settings-log-actions-source-audit.txt); [Samsung and host retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0068-share-logs-retest.txt); App Settings ViewModel, Android/iOS FileSharer and FileLogger implementations, and `LogShareOperationTest`. Samsung read/chooser failure injection is not run.
- **Root cause:** Platform abstractions previously swallowed failures; the ViewModel emitted a success-implying event before invoking the chooser and had no failure/retry state.
- **Affected files:** App Settings ViewModel/state/screen and Analytics API/event sanitizer; FileSharer contract/platform implementations; FileLogger read contract/platform implementations where required; host tests and QA evidence/results.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `9acf3381be57bc96f64e05ad5d610dcc71d7f3f6` (`fix(settings): report log-share outcomes [QA-BUG-0068]`). It replaces the premature `logs_shared` emission with `log_share_attempted`, `log_share_unavailable`, `log_share_sheet_launch_succeeded`, `log_share_failed`, and `log_share_cancelled`; adds correlated diagnostics/retry feedback; and propagates platform read/share exceptions without exposing file contents or paths.
- **Retest:** Host PASS: all 33 Home UI host tests (0 failures/errors/skips), including five `LogShareOperationTest` cases for empty file, launch success, read exception, chooser exception and cancellation with no non-fatal. Android debug assembly and iOS Simulator Kotlin compilation of `base-ui`, Analytics implementation and Home UI passed; iOS runtime tests were not run. Samsung PASS for a temporary synthetic non-sensitive log (chooser became foreground; System Back returned to Parrot; no recipient selected) and for empty logs (no chooser; “No logs to share” shown). Temporary file removed and verified absent; no app data cleared and logging preferences unchanged. Samsung injected failure variants remain BLOCKED without safe fixtures. DebugAnalyticsManager events/breadcrumbs were local only; Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived, Crashlytics backend delivery is unverified. The system chooser dismissal/recipient-selection outcome is tracked separately as QA-BUG-0070.

## QA-BUG-0069 — Clear Logs can confirm success when deletion failed

- **Severity / user impact:** Medium; users can believe all diagnostic data was cleared even though the active log, rotated backup log, or both remain on device. This breaks the stated privacy/recovery expectation and produces misleading success telemetry.
- **Status:** FIXED in the current source tree; host failure/postcondition regressions and Samsung active+rotated/missing-log retests PASS. Filesystem failure injection remains BLOCKED on Samsung.
- **Screen/test IDs:** App Settings case 434; extensions 723–725/clear; observability case 749.
- **Device/build/commit:** Pre-fix audit on Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; installed app 0.4.5 (21), PID `11251`, APK SHA-256 `75e418871ec005041a2f01259c080662bf4009d76aca7bef3763fd34f8916fd3`. Fixed retest on same device/package/version, PID `9345`, APK SHA-256 `2f3554db7a8397129859b140de2ea896f21c3588d168661e048760b1c5714801`; tested source tree committed as `c163e9fee9e18317c1314390d8e8939d6933ef8f`. The APK was built before commit from the identical fix tree plus unrelated pre-existing changes. No raw log contents or user/profile values were retained.
- **Preconditions:** File logging has created a nonempty active log and optionally a rotated backup `parrot_logs_old.txt`; filesystem deletion may also fail.
- **Exact reproduction / audit:** Pre-fix source trace showed `AppSettingsViewModel.clearLogs()` unconditionally called `fileLogger.clearLogs()`, emitted `LogsCleared` and showed success. Both platform implementations targeted only `parrot_logs.txt`, while rotation explicitly stores `parrot_logs_old.txt`; Android ignored `File.delete()` results and both platforms suppressed failures. The clear contract exposed no verified postcondition. Fixed Samsung repro used only temporary synthetic active+rotated files, tapped Clear Logs, and verified both names absent; a second tap with both missing also succeeded.
- **Expected:** Clear both active and rotated log files, then verify both are absent before emitting the existing success event/snackbar. If either deletion fails or remains, present retryable failure, emit one bounded failure event/breadcrumb and report one contextual non-fatal. Missing files are an ordinary successful no-op. Never log file contents or paths.
- **Actual (pre-fix):** Platform clear removed only the active log and swallowed deletion results/errors; ViewModel always emitted `logs_cleared` and success feedback. **Fixed:** Android/iOS remove both active and rotated log files and verify both absent; platform failures propagate as a generic path-free error. ViewModel emits attempt then exactly one terminal success/failure/cancel outcome, with retry feedback and one contextual non-fatal for unexpected failure. A successful clear breadcrumb is sent to the diagnostic provider but not persisted back into the log file being cleared.
- **Frequency:** Every normal clear when a rotated backup exists leaves it behind; deletion error behavior is deterministic but not induced at runtime.
- **Evidence:** [Sanitized source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/app-settings-log-actions-source-audit.txt); [Samsung and host retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0069-clear-logs-retest.txt); App Settings `LogsClearOperationTest`, analytics `DiagnosticLogFilesTest`, Android/iOS FileLogger implementations and the FileLogger contract. Samsung failure injection was not run.
- **Root cause:** The clear operation targets only the active log despite a rotated backup, exposes no result/postcondition contract, suppresses platform errors and is treated as success by the ViewModel.
- **Affected files:** App Settings ViewModel/state/screen and Analytics event sanitizer; `FileLogger` contract and Android/iOS implementations (active and backup deletion); host tests and QA evidence/results.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `c163e9fee9e18317c1314390d8e8939d6933ef8f` (`fix(settings): verify clearing diagnostic logs [QA-BUG-0069]`). It updates `FileLogger.clearLogs()` to delete+verify active and rotated files, preserves platform errors without exposing paths, adds `logs_clear_attempted`/`logs_cleared`/`logs_clear_failed`/`logs_clear_cancelled` with retry and correlated diagnostic outcomes, and makes failures retryable in App Settings.
- **Retest:** PASS: Android host suites reported 14 suites/82 tests, 0 failures/errors/skips; targeted tests cover active+backup deletion, missing-file no-op, false delete/postcondition failure, exception redaction, retry, one contextual exception, cancellation and no log recreation from the terminal breadcrumb. Android debug assembly and iOS Simulator Kotlin compilation of Base UI, Analytics implementation and Home UI passed; iOS runtime tests were not run. Samsung synthetic active+rotated fixtures both disappeared after Clear Logs and the UI showed “Logs cleared”; a second missing-file clear also passed. Preferences unchanged; fixtures absent afterward; no data clear. On-device deletion-failure injection remains BLOCKED. DebugAnalyticsManager events/breadcrumbs were local only; Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived and Crashlytics delivery is unverified.
- **Ordered case-434 retest:** PASS on Samsung PID `21569`, APK SHA-256 `310dda0fdc9201b2815e0d855afb94e677b15d496b192087f8d183723868936e`. Cleared the active 2,748-byte app-generated log from cases 430/431; “Logs cleared” appeared, and metadata checks confirmed active plus backup absent afterward, with no terminal-breadcrumb recreation. Exactly one local `logs_clear_attempted` + `logs_cleared` and matching start/success breadcrumbs; file logging remained OFF, no app data cleared. [Ordered evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/case-434-clear-logs.txt). Failure injection remains BLOCKED.

## QA-BUG-0070 — Dismissing the system Share Logs chooser has no Analytics outcome

- **Severity / user impact:** Low functional impact; medium measurement gap. The app can count a share-sheet launch but cannot distinguish a user who cancels/back-dismisses from one who chooses a recipient, so share completion and abandonment denominators are misleading.
- **Status:** BLOCKED after Samsung confirmation and Android API/source audit. Functional return is verified, but reliable chooser-dismissal/abandonment telemetry has no implemented lifecycle/result integration; Analytics variant remains FAIL. No recipient-selection or file-delivery outcome is claimed.
- **Screen/test IDs:** App Settings Share Logs; case 432 launch; `740/AppSettings/ShareLogs/system-back`; observability case 749; case 725/share.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0. First observation: PID `30017`, APK `c1c120b592b8a66ff86cd01a742fcd369fc63fac97678e53880969aacc07fcf1`, source `9acf3381be57bc96f64e05ad5d610dcc71d7f3f6` plus unrelated changes. Ordered case-432 repeat: PID `21569`, APK `310dda0fdc9201b2815e0d855afb94e677b15d496b192087f8d183723868936e`, source includes QA-BUG-0072 commit `85041fee0ab5b0431c5361429dedb3bdb6be2e27` plus unrelated pre-existing changes. Both device/local APK hashes matched.
- **Preconditions:** A nonempty log file is available and Share Logs launches Android's system chooser.
- **Exact reproduction:** On the Samsung, invoke Share Logs from App Settings using the temporary sanitized QA fixture, wait for `com.android.intentresolver/.ChooserActivityLauncher`, then press System Back without choosing a target. Parrot returns to MainActivity/App Settings. Filtered app logs show `log_share_attempted` followed by `log_share_sheet_launch_succeeded`; there is no dismissal/cancel/abandoned event. No recipient was selected and no file was sent.
- **Expected:** Record a bounded, distinct user-dismissed/cancelled outcome where the platform can reliably report it; never infer a recipient or delivery from launch. Ordinary cancellation must not create a Crashlytics non-fatal.
- **Actual:** `AndroidFileSharer.shareFile()` returns as soon as `Context.startActivity()` accepts the chooser request. The common contract has no result callback, so System Back/dismissal and recipient selection cannot be observed. The typed `LogShareCancelled` event covers only an explicit coroutine cancellation exception; Android chooser dismissal does not produce that callback. Android's optional `Intent.EXTRA_CHOSEN_COMPONENT_INTENT_SENDER` reports a successfully selected target, not a cancellation callback ([Android Intent reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/Intent)); the Samsung Sharesheet likewise returned to Parrot after Back without a cancellation event. A lifecycle/result redesign has not been implemented or validated, and a resume-only heuristic cannot safely distinguish Back from selecting a recipient app and returning.
- **Frequency:** Every user dismissal/return that does not choose a destination is unclassified; Samsung reproduced on two separate runs (2/2), including ordered case 432.
- **Evidence:** [Original targeted Samsung/host retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0068-share-logs-retest.txt); [ordered current-build case 432 repeat](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/case-432-share-logs-chooser.txt). Both local event sequences contain attempt + chooser-launch success but no terminal cancellation after Back. No private chooser targets or raw hierarchy are retained.
- **Root cause:** `FileSharer` is a fire-and-forget platform API invoked from application context; chooser lifecycle/result is not propagated to the App Settings operation boundary. The public selected-component callback does not signal dismissal, and a lifecycle heuristic would misclassify valid target switches/returns.
- **Affected files:** `base-ui` Android file sharing and its platform contract; App Settings log-share operation/event model and targeted host/device evidence. Do not capture or log selected component identity.
- **Fix reference / commit:** None; remediation BLOCKED pending a lifecycle-aware platform contract that reliably reports chooser dismissal without logging selected-component identity. Do not emit a cancellation based only on `onResume` or claim recipient/delivery success from the selected-component callback.
- **Retest:** System Back variant executed 2/2: functional PASS (chooser closes and Parrot returns); Analytics FAIL/BLOCKED (only attempt and chooser-launch events, no cancellation/abandonment outcome). Latest case 432 used the genuine sanitized log created in cases 430/431, chose no recipient and sent no file; no app data cleared. No failure/non-fatal expected or observed. This blocked instrumentation variant may not be marked PASS. Firebase ingestion is waived and local debug logs are not delivery evidence; Crashlytics backend delivery remains unverified.

## QA-BUG-0071 — Continue Reading shelf is omitted for filtered-empty Books results

- **Severity / user impact:** Low; when an active Books filter or search has no matches, users cannot resume the current book from the Books shelf even though Continue Reading is enabled and the floating bubble remains available on eligible other screens.
- **Status:** FIXED AND SAMSUNG-RETESTED PASS (`1884404a3660fffeced85756af0f693eea8a01e9`). The pre-fix case-425 shelf failure and preserved off/on Analytics/bubble observations remain documented.
- **Screen/test IDs:** App Settings case 425; related Continue Reading bubble cases 438–439; observability case 749.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), PID `20688`, pre-fix APK SHA-256 `2f3554db7a8397129859b140de2ea896f21c3588d168661e048760b1c5714801`; tested source baseline `f1c8e4d36c16fbc0b71ee2ed91f153e10076d44f` plus the later fix and unrelated pre-existing changes.
- **Preconditions:** App Settings → Show Continue Reading enabled; a current-reading model exists; Books has an active filter/search yielding no results.
- **Exact reproduction:** On Samsung, toggle Show Continue Reading off, open Series and observe the bubble absent; return and toggle on; open Books with the existing one-filter state. The Books screen shows “No Books Match Your Filters” but no Continue Reading shelf. Open Series and the bubble is visible again. The filter and book/profile/server state were not modified.
- **Expected:** The current-reading shelf is restored on Books when the preference is enabled, even if filtered results are empty; eligible other-screen bubble is also restored.
- **Actual:** Other-screen bubble hides/restores correctly, but the Books shelf is not composed when `filteredBooks` is empty; the user loses that resume entry point under the empty-filter state.
- **Frequency:** Reproduced once in one off/on cycle; confirmed by source branch structure. No population frequency inferred.
- **Evidence:** [Sanitized Samsung pre-fix reproduction and source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0071-case-425-filtered-shelf-prefx.txt).
- **Root cause:** `BooksListScreenContent` renders `EmptyBooksState` in the filtered-empty branch without invoking the supplied `headerContent`; HomeNavigation supplies the Continue Reading shelf via that header. The header is included only in nonempty list/grid rendering.
- **Affected files:** `feature/books/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/books/ui/list/BooksListScreen.kt`, relevant Books UI host/regression tests, and QA evidence/results/run report.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `1884404a3660fffeced85756af0f693eea8a01e9` (`fix(books): preserve resume shelf in empty state [QA-BUG-0071]`). `BooksListScreenContent` now composes the supplied header before the weighted empty-state content when filtered results are empty and the screen is not loading; search/filter state is unchanged. Two host regressions cover the empty/header branch and loading/nonempty/no-header exclusions. Installed APK was built immediately before the commit from the identical code tree.
- **Retest:** PASS on Samsung PID `30163`, APK SHA-256 `70fc583c081d3109ab91cd17277ae1c230256695e0422c2c0a772483bea0240e`. With the pre-existing filter untouched, OFF hid both Books shelf and Series bubble; ON restored both, and Books retained the filtered-empty message. Original preference restored ON; app/profile/server/book data unchanged. Host tests PASS (4 tests, 0 failures/errors/skips), Android debug assembly PASS, iOS Simulator Kotlin compile PASS. No exception/fatal expected or observed; no Crashlytics non-fatal is needed for ordinary filtering/toggle behavior. Firebase ingestion is waived; Crashlytics delivery is unverified. [Post-fix retest evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0071-case-425-filtered-shelf-retest.txt); [preserved pre-fix evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0071-case-425-filtered-shelf-prefx.txt).

## QA-BUG-0072 — Sync & Backup route has no screen exposure event or diagnostic breadcrumb

- **Severity / user impact:** Medium instrumentation gap; Cloud Account opens functionally, but the app cannot measure entry into this core account/backup surface or reconstruct its navigation route from production Analytics/diagnostic history.
- **Status:** FIXED and Samsung-retested; fix commit `85041fee0ab5b0431c5361429dedb3bdb6be2e27`. The original pre-fix observation remains preserved; post-fix case 429 passes functionally and locally for route Analytics/diagnostics. Firebase Analytics ingestion and Crashlytics backend delivery remain unverified as specified below.
- **Screen/test IDs:** App Settings case 429; Cloud Account cases 357–384; shared cases 740, 747, 749.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; pre-fix package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), PID `30163`, APK SHA-256 `70fc583c081d3109ab91cd17277ae1c230256695e0422c2c0a772483bea0240e`; post-fix package 0.4.5 (21), PID `21569`, APK SHA-256 `310dda0fdc9201b2815e0d855afb94e677b15d496b192087f8d183723868936e`, built from source HEAD `8f8206594e8cc6ba610816aba50f940dbdf33b61` plus the fix and unrelated pre-existing changes. Local/device hashes matched.
- **Preconditions:** App Settings hub visible; Cloud Account signed-out form is displayed; no form input/account action.
- **Exact reproduction:** Tap Sync & Backup in App Settings. Cloud Account renders. Inspect the app PID's local debug output: no Cloud Account route exposure event or visible-route breadcrumb appears. No credential was entered and no account/server/profile/book state was mutated.
- **Expected:** Exactly one actual-visible-route event and one bounded visible-route breadcrumb with stable screen/source/entry-point values (`sync_and_backup`, `app_settings`, `sync_backup_row` or consistently documented equivalent), never form/account values; route event emitted at the navigation boundary and not on recomposition.
- **Actual:** Before fix, route opened but neither signal was emitted. After fix, one route entry emitted one typed screen exposure and one visible breadcrumb with bounded `screen=sync_and_backup`, `source_screen=app_settings`, `entry_point=sync_backup_row`; no account data. The Samsung run observed exactly one of each.
- **Frequency:** Pre-fix reproduced once on Samsung and confirmed by source audit; post-fix repeated once on Samsung with one event/breadcrumb pair.
- **Evidence:** [Sanitized pre-fix run/source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0072-case-429-sync-backup-route-prefx.txt); [post-fix Samsung retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0072-case-429-sync-backup-route-retest.txt).
- **Root cause:** `HomeNavigation` reported actual destination exposure for Statistics, Server Management and App Settings, but had no branch for `HomeDestination.SyncAndBackup`; Cloud Account's UI module had no route-exposure event/diagnostic contract.
- **Affected files:** `feature/home/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/home/ui/navigation/HomeNavigation.kt`, `HomeNavigationViewModel.kt`, `SyncAndBackupExposureContext.kt`, `SyncAndBackupExposureContextTest.kt`, and `lib/analytics/api/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/analytics/api/BookAnalyticsEvent.kt`. No Cloud Account module dependency change was needed.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `85041fee0ab5b0431c5361429dedb3bdb6be2e27` (`fix(analytics): report Sync & Backup route exposure [QA-BUG-0072, case 429]`). The navigation owner's actual-visible-destination effect now resolves bounded source/entry context, emits typed `sync_and_backup_screen_viewed`, and logs a `sync_and_backup_route` visible breadcrumb; three host tests cover bounded attribution and exact event schema.
- **Retest:** PASS on physical Samsung PID `21569`, installed APK SHA-256 `310dda0fdc9201b2815e0d855afb94e677b15d496b192087f8d183723868936e`. Host suite: 40 tests, 0 failures/errors/skips; 3 focused exposure-context tests passed. iOS Simulator Kotlin compilation and Android debug assembly passed. Update install preserved app data; Cloud Account signed-out form opened; one route exposure produced exactly one local event and one breadcrumb. Toolbar Back returned to App Settings with a correlated navigation pair. No credentials or identifiers entered/retained. Firebase ingestion waived; local output is not Firebase delivery proof. No error induced; Crashlytics backend delivery remains unverified.

## QA-BUG-0073 — Android file-logger write failures may be silently dropped

- **Severity / user impact:** Low (suspected support/diagnostics degradation); with file logging enabled, an I/O failure may leave the preference ON while the promised diagnostic file is empty or incomplete, undermining later troubleshooting.
- **Status:** SUSPECTED by source audit only; no runtime failure has been reproduced. Verification is BLOCKED pending a safe deterministic Android `filesDir` write-failure fixture. Do not claim a confirmed device defect or a fix.
- **Record commit:** `95e9d30af24b23ca8d245a71bd019ac957c6e33b` records the source-audit suspicion and safe-fixture blocker; it contains no code fix.
- **Screen/test IDs:** App Settings cases 430–434; shared log-write/share/clear coverage 725, 740, 747, 749.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; Parrot 0.4.5 (21), PID `21569`, APK SHA-256 `310dda0fdc9201b2815e0d855afb94e677b15d496b192087f8d183723868936e`; source commit `85041fee0ab5b0431c5361429dedb3bdb6be2e27` plus unrelated work. The installed source path is unchanged in this build.
- **Preconditions:** File Logging ON; an Analytics diagnostic breadcrumb or handled exception attempts to append/rotate the file.
- **Exact reproduction/audit:** Source inspection of `AndroidFileLogger.appendToLogFile()` (68–77) and `rotateLogFile()` (80–90) shows all `Exception`s suppressed with no observable result; `FileLogger.log()` returns `Unit`. A safe failure trigger is unavailable, so no runtime reproduction has been attempted.
- **Expected:** Best-effort logging should not crash Parrot, but a bounded non-recursive diagnostic or operation outcome should distinguish a failed file write from a successful log write.
- **Actual:** The source catches and ignores write/rotation exceptions. Runtime/user-visible effect remains unconfirmed.
- **Frequency:** All exceptions entering those catch blocks are suppressed; real-world incidence unknown.
- **Evidence:** [Sanitized source audit and safe-fixture blocker](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-28/qa-bug-0073-file-logger-write-failure-source-audit.txt).
- **Root cause:** `AndroidFileLogger` treats every append/rotation exception as a silent no-op; the FileLogger contract cannot return/report an outcome and there is no alternate non-recursive failure signal.
- **Affected files:** `lib/analytics/implementation/src/androidMain/kotlin/com/retro99/analytics/implementation/AndroidFileLogger.kt`, `lib/analytics/api/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/analytics/api/FileLogger.kt`, safe host/platform tests, and QA evidence/results/run report if confirmed.
- **Fix reference / commit:** None; suspected issue only. No code change until safe failure semantics and validation are agreed by tests.
- **Retest:** Case 430 normal path PASS on Samsung PID `21569`: toggle ON caused the app-private file to appear and grow as diagnostic breadcrumbs were emitted; original preference was restored OFF and the sanitized app-generated file is retained for cases 432/434. This does **not** confirm the suspected failure behavior. Append/rotation failure remains BLOCKED/NOT RUN pending an injectable, safe logger write-failure test seam. Do not exhaust storage, change app-private permissions or corrupt device data to induce failure.

## QA-BUG-0074 — App Settings preference toggles lack operation diagnostics and write-failure recovery

- **Severity / user impact:** Medium instrumentation/recovery gap. A failed local preference write may leave a setting unchanged without a setting-specific breadcrumb or clear retry feedback; the actual failure behavior has not been induced.
- **Status:** FIXED in source and Samsung normal-path retest PASS. Runtime preference-write failure was not reproduced on Samsung; host fault-injection/retry regression passes.
- **Screen/test IDs:** App Settings cases 424–425, 430–431, 436, 719 and 724; four direct toggles: Open Last Book, Show Continue Reading, File Logging, Only Log Crashes.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), PID `21569` before restart / `23213` after, installed APK SHA-256 `310dda0fdc9201b2815e0d855afb94e677b15d496b192087f8d183723868936e`. The installed APK predates repository HEAD `0119c90891ed39438eab48938e25b40abca20c43`.
- **Preconditions:** Reach App Settings and change any of the four Boolean preferences. A thrown `Preferences.putBoolean` is the failure condition; safe Samsung failure injection is not available.
- **Exact reproduction/audit:** Case 436 toggled all four settings and performed a force-stop/relaunch. The values persisted, but the local log showed only the existing setting Analytics events and App Settings route/observer breadcrumbs. Source inspection of `AppSettingsViewModel.setLoggingEnabled`, `setLogCrashesOnly`, `setOpenLastBookOnLaunch`, and `setShowContinueReading` (lines 506–527) confirms each direct `putBoolean` is followed by a setting event and state update, with no operation breadcrumb, exception boundary, or user-visible write-failure recovery. No exception was induced on Samsung.
- **Expected:** Each accepted setting write has bounded attempt and terminal diagnostics correlated to one operation. An unexpected write failure is reported once with safe setting context, does not emit a success event or falsely update UI state, and gives the user visible retry guidance. Successful existing toggle-event names remain stable; retries are distinguishable; cancellation is not misreported as an error.
- **Actual:** Normal success emitted the typed Boolean setting events and persisted across restart; no per-toggle diagnostic context exists. Failure and recovery behavior is unknown at runtime.
- **Frequency:** Every toggle; diagnostic gap occurs on each successful change. User impact occurs only if a write fails.
- **Evidence:** [Case 436 pre-fix run and source-audit result](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/case-436-settings-persistence-prefx.txt); source at `feature/home/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/home/ui/appsettings/AppSettingsViewModel.kt:506-527`.
- **Root cause:** Four legacy setters assume synchronous preference persistence succeeds and only emit a setting-specific Analytics event after the write; diagnostics and write-error feedback were not added with those events.
- **Affected files:** App Settings ViewModel/state/screen/intents, typed Analytics event definitions and sanitizer allowlist, focused common host regression tests, English settings resource, and QA evidence/results/run report.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `2fed51411d5a72765d1151e5f8d4359b086112c9` (`fix(settings): report toggle persistence outcomes [QA-BUG-0074, case 436]`). The focused implementation adds generic typed attempt/failure/cancellation events, retry attribution on existing success events, correlated diagnostic start/terminal breadcrumbs, one contextual non-fatal at the write boundary, unchanged state on failure, and a user-visible retry hint.
- **Retest:** PASS for host normal/failure/retry/cancellation tests, 44 Home UI host tests, 46 Analytics implementation host tests, and `:androidApp:assembleDebug`. On Samsung, all four settings persisted across force-stop/relaunch, original values were restored, and each of eight successful toggles emitted one attempt/success event pair and matching correlated breadcrumbs; no toggle failure/cancellation event occurred. APK SHA-256 `610e505a11d51c11ed64ff3c0a06aad12f8091b3c0c822d99625c3c32a5c0b7e` matched local and pulled device APK. Firebase ingestion remains waived; Samsung preference-write failure and Crashlytics delivery remain unverified. See [case 436 retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/case-436-settings-persistence-retest.txt).

## QA-BUG-0075 — Coil image errors are all reported as non-fatal exceptions

- **Severity / user impact:** Low direct functionality impact; potentially noisy exception reporting can obscure actionable failures and generate unnecessary diagnostic volume.
- **Status:** FIXED in source; host policy/de-duplication tests and Samsung expected-network-error retest PASS. Firebase Crashlytics delivery was not verified.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Case 436 post-fix retest; shared image rendering on Books/Reader/other screens that use `CoilImage`.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; pre-fix `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), PID `4301`, APK SHA-256 `610e505a11d51c11ed64ff3c0a06aad12f8091b3c0c822d99625c3c32a5c0b7e`; fix retest PID `16972`, same version, APK SHA-256 `58ebc8fd43260cc20ea6f125c3c52d1ff1fe62dbe3023652fa8022a2867623aa`, source commit `2fed51411d5a72765d1151e5f8d4359b086112c9` plus QA-BUG-0075 candidate.
- **Preconditions:** Any image request through shared `CoilImage` reaches `AsyncImagePainter.State.Error`; ordinary HTTP/network rejection and cancellation are included.
- **Exact reproduction:** On first post-update launch with Open Last Book ON, the known unauthenticated server route was closed without retry. Pre-fix PID-scoped local debug output recorded two sanitized `HttpException` reports originating in Coil's Ktor `NetworkFetcher`. Source inspection confirmed `CoilImage.kt` unconditionally reported each `AsyncImagePainter.State.Error`. On the fixed build, reran the same retained route/fixture and dismissed the expected prompt with Close; Books recovered, with zero generic exception reports, `HttpException` strings, shared-image diagnostics, or fatal markers from the new PID. No raw log, URL, cache key, or book/profile identity was retained.
- **Expected:** Do not report ordinary cancellation or expected HTTP/network image failures as Crashlytics non-fatals. Report only an unexpected, materially user-impacting error once with bounded, payload-free context.
- **Actual (pre-fix):** Every `AsyncImagePainter.State.Error` was sent to the exception-reporting API without classification or de-duplication; runtime saw two such reports. **After fix:** expected HTTP/network/cancellation errors are suppressed; unexpected errors are reported with a bounded `DiagnosticContext` at most once per remembered image request, with no URL or cache key.
- **Frequency:** Potentially every failed image request on every screen using the component; repeat behavior depends on request lifecycle.
- **Evidence:** [Sanitized source/runtime audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/qa-bug-0075-coil-image-failure-source-runtime-audit.txt); `base-ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/base/ui/compose/CoilImage.kt:43-52`.
- **Root cause:** Shared image UI treats all painter failures as unexpected exceptions and has no expected-network classification or duplicate suppression.
- **Affected files:** `base-ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/base/ui/compose/CoilImage.kt`, image failure reporting policy, `base-ui/build.gradle.kts` common test dependency, common policy tests, and QA evidence/results/run report.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `87029a420a6b55441a1e977d67755bb1378dfe3b` (`fix(images): filter expected image failures [QA-BUG-0075]`). The fix suppresses cancellation, Coil `HttpException`, and `kotlinx.io.IOException` anywhere in the cause chain; it reports other errors once per image request with bounded fields and no free-form/cache-key context.
- **Retest:** PASS: 7 iOS Simulator common tests (expected HTTP, nested HTTP, I/O, cancellation, unexpected decode errors, one-shot reporting, and expected-then-unexpected state); Android debug assembly PASS. Final Samsung debug APK `58ebc8fd43260cc20ea6f125c3c52d1ff1fe62dbe3023652fa8022a2867623aa` matched local and pulled device copies. Same retained unauthenticated current-book route reached the known failure prompt and safely returned to Books with no image exception report/fatal marker. Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived; local provider output is not Crashlytics delivery evidence. See [Samsung retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/qa-bug-0075-coil-image-failure-retest.txt).

## QA-BUG-0076 — Floating Continue Reading position writes can fail silently

- **Title:** Bubble drag position has no write-failure reporting or recovery.
- **Severity / user impact:** Medium, recoverable state loss. If local preference serialization/storage throws after a drag, the bubble may return to its old position after restart without telling the user or offering a retry; reading itself remains available.
