- **Status:** FIXED in source; host injected failure/retry regression and Samsung normal drag/save/persistence retest PASS. Samsung preference-write failure injection remains BLOCKED.
- **Retest device/build:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), PIDs `24042` / `28046`, APK SHA-256 `a461b3dfa4531ac4ea1b556fc2e8205f5d67cc80a637d1d798be41456779fe24`, source/fix commit `cbb08da20c9da3bb5562d0954171053d018bd7fa`.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Home/navigation case 438; extensions 543, 748–749, 773 and 775.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), PID `16972`, debug APK SHA-256 `58ebc8fd43260cc20ea6f125c3c52d1ff1fe62dbe3023652fa8022a2867623aa`, source commit `87029a420a6b55441a1e977d67755bb1378dfe3b`; repository HEAD is docs-only `0d728e1df550b42355d329ede0e6b2b66de0affa`.
- **Preconditions:** Bubble enabled, current-reading model available, and bubble-position preference observable. A thrown `Preferences.putString` during `saveUserPreferenceUseCase` is the failure condition; no safe Samsung fault fixture is currently available.
- **Pre-fix reproduction/source audit:** Drag the bubble and release. `DraggableFloatingBubble.onDragEnd` called `UpdateBubblePosition`; `HomeNavigationViewModel.saveBubblePosition` serialized and synchronously wrote the preference without try/catch, Analytics outcome, breadcrumb, non-fatal, or UI recovery. No preference-write exception has been induced on-device.
- **Expected:** Each committed move produces bounded attempted and terminal outcomes with a correlation ID in diagnostics, side only as a bounded Analytics dimension, and no raw coordinates. On unexpected persistence failure, do not emit success, report one contextual non-fatal, preserve safe state, show an actionable retry, and allow a separately attributed retry. Gesture cancellation is ordinary cancellation and is not reported as a failure.
- **Pre-fix actual:** The view animated to the released position and invoked a synchronous preference write. A thrown write propagated without a bubble-specific boundary or user-facing feedback. Runtime failure remains unconfirmed; post-fix normal/failure behavior is recorded under Retest.
- **Frequency:** Every completed bubble drag; user impact only when serialization/storage fails.
- **Evidence:** [Case-438 pre-fix source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/case-438-bubble-instrumentation-source-audit.txt); `DraggableFloatingBubble.kt` and `HomeNavigationViewModel.kt`.
- **Root cause:** Position persistence was wired directly to the preference use case without an operation outcome/recovery wrapper.
- **Affected files:** Home navigation state/viewmodel/screen, typed navigation Analytics and sanitizer, focused host tests.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `cbb08da20c9da3bb5562d0954171053d018bd7fa` (`fix(home): report bubble position save outcomes [QA-BUG-0076, 438]`). Added correlated attempted/succeeded/failed/cancelled events and breadcrumbs; one contextual non-fatal for unexpected write failure; retry snackbar; host fault-injection/retry coverage and bounded sanitizer tests.
- **Documentation/history commit:** `d114c71ea56213f9c02e3b60010e4e9c54145cb1` records the QA-BUG-0076 Samsung normal-path retest and preserves the original source-audit result.
- **Retest:** Host suite PASS: 94 Home UI + Analytics implementation tests total, 0 failures/errors/skips, including simulated preference exception, one failure report, separately attributed successful retry, and cancellation-without-exception. Android debug assembly PASS. Samsung drag to left edge snapped correctly; local Analytics had one attempt/success pair and matching correlated start/terminal breadcrumbs. Force-stop/relaunch restored left-side position. Bubble returned near the original right position after the run. No preference failure was injected on device; Crashlytics delivery unverified. [Samsung evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/case-438-bubble-drag-samsung.txt).

## QA-BUG-0077 — Continue Reading opens lack source-attributed attempt and terminal telemetry

- **Title:** Floating/shelf Continue Reading navigation is not measured as a source-specific operation.
- **Severity / user impact:** Low direct functionality impact; successful usage, abandonment, cancellation and failure rates for this entry point cannot be distinguished, and failure breadcrumbs cannot reconstruct its source.
- **Status:** FIXED in source; host event/sanitizer tests and Samsung bubble+shelf expected-auth-failure attribution retest PASS. Successful usable-content, explicit Retry, playback-conflict, audiobook, and unexpected Crashlytics-delivery variants remain unverified or blocked.
- **Pre-fix Samsung observation:** QA-BUG-0076 candidate `cbb08da20c9da3bb5562d0954171053d018bd7fa`, package 0.4.5 (21), PID `28046`, APK SHA-256 `a461b3dfa4531ac4ea1b556fc2e8205f5d67cc80a637d1d798be41456779fe24`; bubble tap reached the unauthenticated Reader prompt, and PID-filtered output had zero source-open events. Close only; no Retry or fixture change.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Home/navigation cases 81, 438–439; extensions 542–545, 563, 565, 720, 747–749 and 774–775.
- **Pre-fix device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), PID `16972`, debug APK SHA-256 `58ebc8fd43260cc20ea6f125c3c52d1ff1fe62dbe3023652fa8022a2867623aa`, source commit `87029a420a6b55441a1e977d67755bb1378dfe3b`.
- **Preconditions:** A visible Continue Reading shelf or bubble and a current-reading target. A usable authenticated/cached content fixture is required to verify success; a controlled unavailable target can verify safe failure/recovery without changing the retained fixture.
- **Exact reproduction/source audit:** Tap the bubble/shelf. Both dispatch the same `RequestOpenReader` as other sources; no bubble/shelf entry point is carried into navigation. A possible playback-conflict dialog has no source-attributed cancel result. Existing reader `book_opened`/`book_open_failed` events have no Continue Reading entry point; non-startup failure diagnostics lack source/correlation context. `ContinueReadingLaunched(bookUuid)` is defined but unused and the sanitizer drops `book_uuid`. Samsung pre-fix tap after QA-BUG-0076 install opened the Reader route and showed the retained unauthenticated-server prompt; Close (not Retry) returned to Series. PID-filtered local Analytics showed 0 `continue_reading_open` and 0 `continue_reading_launched` events. No raw content/log lines retained. This runtime observation corroborates, but does not replace, the source audit.
- **Expected:** Emit one bounded attempt when a Continue Reading action is accepted, then one terminal succeeded only after usable Reader content, failed on a terminal open error, or cancelled on conflict-dialog dismissal/close before usable content. Retry is a separate attempt. Breadcrumbs carry a diagnostic-only correlation ID. Never include titles, book/profile IDs, URLs, content or free-form errors; expected auth/offline rejection and ordinary cancellation do not create Crashlytics issues.
- **Pre-fix actual:** No source-specific attempt, completion, cancellation or correlation record was emitted by the Continue Reading entry path. Existing reader success/failure signals could not be attributed to this source; post-fix observations are in Retest.
- **Frequency:** Every shelf or bubble open, with the gap on each attempt/outcome.
- **Evidence:** [Case-438 pre-fix source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/case-438-bubble-instrumentation-source-audit.txt) and [Samsung drag/open observation](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/case-438-bubble-drag-samsung.txt); `HomeNavigation.kt`, `HomeNavigationViewModel.kt`, and `ReaderViewModel.kt`.
- **Root cause:** Continue Reading reuses the generic book-open route and the defined launch event has no call site; route source is not propagated to Reader.
- **Affected files:** Home navigation intent/state/routes and viewmodel; Reader and Android audiobook route parameters/viewmodels; typed Analytics API and bounded provider sanitizer; source-attribution, retry/cancellation and sanitizer tests.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `ee46e6deebab167250c287978066937717ba4278` (`fix(reader): attribute Continue Reading open outcomes [QA-BUG-0077, 438]`). Carries a bounded shelf/bubble entry point and diagnostic-only correlation through Reader and audiobook routes; emits attempt, usable-content success, auth/network/initialization failure, cancellation, and separately marked retry events. Conflict dismissal is a cancellation. No book/profile IDs or free-form errors were added to the new Analytics schema.
- **Retest:** PASS for source-attributed expected-auth-failure variants on Samsung PID `4402`, package 0.4.5 (21), APK SHA-256 `e9512073b0c83b0fc5eac9ebc2ae33d9f5f17c550b6dcc877ecea0b3cb24d8ac` (local/device hashes match). Bubble and shelf each emitted exactly one local attempted/failed event pair (`server_not_authenticated`) and matching correlated started/terminal breadcrumbs; no fatal/generic exception marker. Close only; no Retry or fixture change. Host suite PASS: 100 Home UI + Analytics implementation tests total, 0 failures/errors/skips; Android debug assembly and iOS source compilation PASS. Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived and not claimed. Successful usable-content open, retry, conflict-dialog cancellation, audiobook interaction, unexpected non-fatal reporting, and Crashlytics delivery remain NOT RUN/BLOCKED. [Samsung retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/case-438-continue-reading-open-samsung.txt).

## QA-BUG-0078 — Continue Reading target leaks across profile switch

- **Title:** Previous profile's Continue Reading shelf/bubble remains visible in a profile with no current-book target.
- **Severity / user impact:** Medium; profile-isolation/privacy and wrong-target risk. The new profile can see and potentially open the prior profile's reading target even though its own Settings state has no current book.
- **Status:** FIXED in source and Samsung-retested. The no-current-book destination state no longer inherits the prior profile's shelf/bubble; switching back restores the original profile's target. Literal Clear Current Book action remains NOT RUN to preserve the original profile.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Home/navigation case 439; profile-switch cases 409 and 776; related visibility/setting cases 425, 438 and 775.
- **Pre-fix device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0, build `BP4A.251205.006.S921BXXSGDZG1`; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), PID `4402`, APK SHA-256 `e9512073b0c83b0fc5eac9ebc2ae33d9f5f17c550b6dcc877ecea0a3bcb24d8ac`, source commit `ee46e6deebab167250c287978066937717ba4278`.
- **Preconditions:** Original active profile has a current-reading target; an existing disposable QA profile has no current-book row in App Settings; Show Continue Reading is enabled.
- **Exact reproduction:** On Samsung, open App Settings and switch from the original profile to the disposable QA profile. On the destination profile's App Settings, observe Show Continue Reading enabled and no Clear Current Book row. Select Series. The floating bubble remains visible; Books also showed the Continue Reading shelf after the switch. Do not tap the stale item. Switch back to the original profile afterward; its saved state remained visible. No profile labels/IDs or book identifiers retained.
- **Expected:** Profile switch clears/reloads current-reading and bubble state for the destination profile before its routes render. A profile with no current book exposes neither shelf nor bubble. The prior profile's target is not visible or routable from the destination profile.
- **Actual:** Destination Settings had no Clear Current Book action, yet Series still displayed a Continue Reading bubble and Books displayed the shelf from the prior profile's Home state.
- **Frequency:** Confirmed once before fix; destination/source profile switch retested successfully after fix.
- **Evidence:** [Pre-fix Samsung observation](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/case-439-profile-current-book-leak.txt); [post-fix Samsung retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/case-439-profile-current-book-rebind-retest.txt).
- **Root cause:** Confirmed. `ObserveUserPreferenceUseCase` scopes its returned preference Flow using the active profile ID at invocation time. The long-lived HomeNavigationViewModel created the current-reading and bubble-position subscriptions once; its existing profile-change handling reset navigation stacks but did not rebind those flows or clear their old values.
- **Affected files:** `feature/home/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/home/ui/navigation/HomeNavigationViewModel.kt`; new `ProfileScopedPreferenceObservation.kt`; `ProfileScopedPreferenceObservationTest.kt`; `lib/analytics/api/.../BookAnalyticsEvent.kt`; bounded Analytics sanitizer regression test.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `690645cd49364ca96cd6cd50a5ae955383278abe` (`fix(home): rebind Continue Reading on profile switch [QA-BUG-0078, 439]`). Re-subscribes current-reading and bubble-position observations on active-profile ID changes, clears stale values before reading the destination profile, and emits bounded profile-state Analytics plus correlated diagnostic breadcrumbs. No profile/book identifiers are included.
- **Retest:** PASS on final Samsung PID `23049`, package 0.4.5 (21), APK SHA-256 `56fe042e3a4bcad5b86a822cb9dbd1199956c7d972aa0169cb493b3fb695ec72` (local and device-pulled hashes match). Empty-profile switch hid both shelf and bubble; switch back restored the original target. Two profile-switch attempt/success event pairs and three profile-state outcomes (initial available, switched unavailable, returned available) were present locally. Three rebind correlation start/terminal pairs matched; no fatal/exception markers. Host tests 103/103 pass; Android debug assembly and iOS Simulator source compilation pass. Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived; Crashlytics unexpected-failure delivery was not exercised. Literal Clear Current Book action remains NOT RUN; original profile/data preserved. [Final candidate evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/case-439-profile-current-book-rebind-retest.txt).

## QA-BUG-0079 — Add Server Login exposure is attributed to Home

- **Title:** Login screen exposure from Server Management Add Server is attributed to Home.
- **Severity / user impact:** Low; navigation-source and Add Server conversion analysis misclassify users, and the diagnostic journey does not identify the actual entry route. No direct functional/data impact.
- **Status:** FIXED, committed and retested on the final combined committed Samsung build.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Server Management cases 443 and 524; shared journey/analytics cases 747 and 749.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0, build `BP4A.251205.006.S921BXXSGDZG1`; package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), final PID `25746`, APK SHA-256 `d1f5202462a20294aa5a8c3fd227266deace01e661dfb37c3973fea0b0fafe06`, source tree HEAD `88f44a5f0d502419413dcf95c7c63ad30c2a651f` (app code includes focused fix commit `8e16676916c7ad06d82bc4bd93ecfd67f172cf80`).
- **Preconditions:** Open Settings → Servers; use the visible Add Server action and do not enter or submit credentials.
- **Exact reproduction:** Tap Add Server once. The destination Login screen becomes visible. Inspect the local `login_screen_viewed` event and `login_route` breadcrumb. The preceding `server_add_attempted` correctly identifies `server_management/add_server_button`, but the Login destination reports a different source.
- **Expected:** The actual Login exposure and matching breadcrumb preserve the bounded source `server_management` and Add Server entry point (for example `add_server_button`); exactly one exposure is recorded after Login is visible.
- **Actual:** `login_screen_viewed` and `login_route` both record `source_screen=home, entry_point=add_server`. No credentials were submitted and no server was registered. `server_added` was not emitted.
- **Frequency:** Same misattribution observed on the committed QA-BUG-0038 retest (`107d017c`, evidence dated 2026-09-27) and reproduced on the current candidate.
- **Evidence:** [Current candidate cases 440–443/524](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cases-440-443-server-management-candidate.txt); [prior case-443 retest showing the same source](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-27/case-443-add-server-telemetry-fixed-retest.txt).
- **Root cause:** Confirmed. `HomeNavigation` forwards Add Server to the root Login destination using only existing-server ID/retry state. `RootDestination.Login` and `RootNavigationIntent.OnLoginClicked` do not carry the originating screen/entry point. `LoginNavigationViewModel` therefore falls back to `startAtLogin → home/add_server` for this non-initial new-server route.
- **Affected files:** Root/Login route source contract (`HomeNavigation`, `RootNavigationIntent`, `RootDestination`, `RootNavigationViewModel`, `LoginNavigationViewModel`) and regression tests; retain the existing correctly attributed `server_add_attempted` event.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `8e16676916c7ad06d82bc4bd93ecfd67f172cf80` (`fix(login): preserve Add Server source attribution [QA-BUG-0079, 443]`). `RootDestination.Login` carries bounded `sourceScreen`/`entryPoint` from `RootNavigationIntent.OnLoginClicked` into `LoginNavigation`; Server Management Add Server supplies `server_management/add_server_button`. `LoginNavigationViewModel` prefers this explicit route origin when reporting actual Login visibility. A Login UI regression test verifies both event and breadcrumb attribution; no server/account IDs are included.
- **Retest:** PASS first on the fix candidate, then on the final combined committed Samsung build. Final run: PID `25746`, package 0.4.5 (21), APK SHA-256 `d1f5202462a20294aa5a8c3fd227266deace01e661dfb37c3973fea0b0fafe06` (local/device hashes match), source tree HEAD `88f44a5f0d502419413dcf95c7c63ad30c2a651f`. Add Server opened Login twice; both actual `login_screen_viewed` events and matching `login_route` breadcrumbs reported `server_management/add_server_button`, alongside two `server_add_attempted`; `server_added=0`, and no credentials were entered. ComposeApp host suite 8/8 and Login UI suite 37/37 passed; Android debug assembly succeeded/up-to-date. [Final combined evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cases-443-524-final-committed-build.txt); [initial fix evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/qa-bug-0079-login-source-retest.txt). Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived; local logs only. No Crashlytics issue is expected for normal navigation.

## QA-BUG-0080 — Login system Back has no navigation outcome telemetry

- **Title:** Returning from the Add Server Login screen to Server Management emits no Back outcome or diagnostic breadcrumb.
- **Severity / user impact:** Low; Login abandonment/return funnels cannot distinguish a completed system-Back navigation from a missing action, and the diagnostic navigation trail is incomplete. Functional Back returned correctly.
- **Status:** FIXED, committed and retested on the final combined committed Samsung build.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Server Management case 443; Login-from-Add-Server Back case 524; shared exits/journey cases 740 and 747.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0, build `BP4A.251205.006.S921BXXSGDZG1`; package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), final PID `25746`, APK SHA-256 `d1f5202462a20294aa5a8c3fd227266deace01e661dfb37c3973fea0b0fafe06`, source tree HEAD `88f44a5f0d502419413dcf95c7c63ad30c2a651f` (app code includes focused fix commit `dd0e6fc721de08b68f59cd6d6a0bf6aa968610f1`).
- **Preconditions:** Open Server Management, tap Add Server, do not enter/submit credentials, then use Android system Back from Login.
- **Exact reproduction:** The Login UI closes and Server Management/card becomes visible. Inspect PID-scoped events and breadcrumbs for `source_screen=login`, `destination_screen=server_management`, `entry_point=system_back`. None is emitted. Source audit shows `RootNavigationViewModel.handleBackFromLogin()` only removes the Login destination from the back stack.
- **Expected:** Record one bounded `navigation_back_attempted` and one applied `navigation_back` success with source, destination and `system_back` entry point, plus correlated start/terminal breadcrumbs. Ordinary Back must not create a Crashlytics issue.
- **Actual:** Functional return succeeds, but there is no Login Back attempt/success Analytics event or navigation Back breadcrumb for this transition. Other Server Management Back paths in the same run did emit their own correctly attributed outcomes.
- **Frequency:** Confirmed once on the current Samsung candidate; prior case-443 capture did not inspect Login Back telemetry.
- **Evidence:** [Current candidate cases 440–443/524](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cases-440-443-server-management-candidate.txt); `RootNavigationViewModel.handleBackFromLogin()` source path.
- **Root cause:** Confirmed. The root Login Back callback directly pops the root navigation stack without invoking the nested Home Back instrumentation or logging a root-level Back outcome.
- **Affected files:** `composeApp/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/parrot/navigation/{RootNavigationIntent.kt,RootNavigationViewModel.kt,RootNavigation.kt}` and root navigation host tests; may share bounded navigation context with QA-BUG-0079 but remains an independent operation/outcome defect.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `dd0e6fc721de08b68f59cd6d6a0bf6aa968610f1` (`fix(navigation): report root Login Back outcomes [QA-BUG-0080, 524]`). `RootNavigationViewModel.handleBackFromLogin()` now ignores stale/duplicate pops, records one typed `navigation_back_attempted`, and records the terminal outcome only after the root Login destination is popped. Start and terminal diagnostics share a generated correlation ID. Root callbacks distinguish `system_back` from `toolbar_back`; a failed applied-pop invariant has one root-boundary non-fatal, while ordinary Back does not.
- **Retest:** System Back PASS on the initial fix candidate and final combined committed Samsung build. Final run: PID `25746`, package 0.4.5 (21), APK SHA-256 `d1f5202462a20294aa5a8c3fd227266deace01e661dfb37c3973fea0b0fafe06`, source tree HEAD `88f44a5f0d502419413dcf95c7c63ad30c2a651f`. Exactly one Login → Server Management `system_back` attempted/success pair and one correlated diagnostic start/terminal pair were observed in the final run; no fatal or non-fatal. ComposeApp host tests 8/8, Login UI host tests 37/37; Android debug assembly succeeded/up-to-date. [Final combined evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cases-443-524-final-committed-build.txt); [initial system Back evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/qa-bug-0080-root-login-back-retest.txt). Firebase ingestion is waived; Crashlytics is not expected for ordinary Back.

## QA-BUG-0081 — Visible Login toolbar Back is not actionable

- **Title:** The visible Back arrow on the Add Server Login form does not respond to touch and is absent from the exposed accessibility hierarchy.
- **Severity / user impact:** Medium; a visible exit affordance is inert, leaving system Back as the only verified way to return from Login. This can trap or confuse users relying on the button or assistive technology.
- **Status:** FIXED, committed and retested on the final combined committed Samsung build.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Login from Server Management, cases 443, 524 and 740; accessibility cross-check 491/745 (not a full TalkBack pass).
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0, build `BP4A.251205.006.S921BXXSGDZG1`; final PID `25746`, package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), APK SHA-256 `d1f5202462a20294aa5a8c3fd227266deace01e661dfb37c3973fea0b0fafe06`, source tree HEAD `88f44a5f0d502419413dcf95c7c63ad30c2a651f`, fix commit `fc01927575d688d2f2172174da11c10e92e3dcc8`.
- **Preconditions:** Settings → Servers → Add Server; leave every Login field empty.
- **Exact reproduction:** The Login form visibly renders the upper-left arrow. Attempt to activate its center at screen coordinates near `(75,188)` using Android touch automation and `adb shell input tap`; the form remains visible and no Back navigation event is emitted. The accessibility/UI hierarchy does not expose a Back node. In the same state, Android system Back returns to Server Management (QA-BUG-0080's system path).
- **Expected:** The visible arrow is a reachable, labelled clickable control; one tap returns to Server Management and routes through the toolbar Back outcome instrumentation.
- **Actual:** Multiple taps at the visible arrow do nothing. The screen remains on Login, and the UI hierarchy exposes no Back control. System Back works.
- **Frequency:** Reproduced in the current candidate session across three coordinate taps plus an accessibility-node lookup; no successful toolbar activation observed.
- **Evidence:** [Candidate screenshot and test record](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/qa-bug-0081-login-toolbar-back-unresponsive.png) and [case 524 candidate record](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/qa-bug-0081-login-toolbar-back-unresponsive.txt).
- **Root cause:** Confirmed. The full-size scrollable `Column` was composed after the Back `IconButton`, covering its pointer/accessibility hit target. Moving the existing labelled Back affordance after the form in the `Box` restores semantics and hit-test priority.
- **Affected files:** `feature/login/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/login/ui/login/LoginScreen.kt`.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `fc01927575d688d2f2172174da11c10e92e3dcc8` (`fix(login): restore toolbar Back hit target [QA-BUG-0081, 524]`). The Back IconButton is now composed above the full-screen scrollable content; its existing localized `Back` label and callback are unchanged.
- **Retest:** PASS on the initial candidate and final combined committed Samsung build. Final run: PID `25746`, APK SHA-256 `d1f5202462a20294aa5a8c3fd227266deace01e661dfb37c3973fea0b0fafe06` (local and device-pulled hashes match), source tree HEAD `88f44a5f0d502419413dcf95c7c63ad30c2a651f`. UI hierarchy exposed one clickable `Back` node; activation returned to Server Management. Exactly one Login → Server Management `toolbar_back` attempted/success pair and one correlated diagnostic start/terminal pair; no fatal marker. ComposeApp host suite 8/8, Login UI host suite 37/37; Android debug assembly succeeded/up-to-date. [Final combined evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cases-443-524-final-committed-build.txt); [initial toolbar retest](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/qa-bug-0081-login-toolbar-back-retest.txt); [pre-fix screenshot](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/qa-bug-0081-login-toolbar-back-unresponsive.png). Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived; Crashlytics is not expected for ordinary Back.

## QA-BUG-0082 — Cloud Account actions have no operation Analytics or diagnostics

- **Title:** Cloud Account form and connected-account operations have no action-level Analytics outcomes or operation diagnostics.
- **Severity / user impact:** Medium; account registration/sign-in, OAuth, profile linking, storage usage, auto-backup, sync enablement, sign-out and deletion cannot be measured as attempts/completions/recovery, while unexpected operation failures are reduced to UI state without contextual Crashlytics reporting. This prevents reliable adoption and failure analysis for the Cloud Account screen.
- **Status:** CONFIRMED by source audit; focused instrumentation is implemented. Existing Sync & Backup route exposure remains a separate navigation owner and this finding does not reclassify that route.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Cloud Account cases 357–384, 730–739 and new case 778; shared cases 740, 742–749.
- **Device/build/commit:** Required Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0, build `BP4A.251205.006.S921BXXSGDZG1`; installed package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), last hash-verified debug APK SHA-256 `d1f5202462a20294aa5a8c3fd227266deace01e661dfb37c3973fea0b0fafe06`, code source through commit `88f44a5f0d502419413dcf95c7c63ad30c2a651f`; repository HEAD at audit `af5d45038ace04fb6b1062f98aa6f5d2e34b1218` (documentation-only continuation commits after app build). No Cloud Account action was executed for this source audit.
- **Preconditions:** Source audit of `CloudAccountScreen`, `CloudAccountViewModel`, feature module dependencies, Analytics API and Cloud Account domain/repository operation graph.
- **Exact reproduction:** Trace intents `OnSubmitClicked`, `OnGoogleSignInClicked`, `OnSyncClicked`, `OnAutoBackupToggled/Confirmed`, `OnLinkConfirmed`, `OnSignOutClicked`, and `OnDeleteAccountConfirmed` into the ViewModel. Inspect for `Analytics.logEvent`, `logBreadcrumb` or contextual `logException` at accepted operation/terminal boundaries.
- **Expected:** Preserve existing one-per-visible-route `sync_and_backup_screen_viewed` and visible-route breadcrumb. Add typed bounded usage events for user-visible feature actions with started/succeeded/failed/cancelled outcomes, plus correlated diagnostic start/stage/terminal records. Report only unexpected user-impacting failures once at the UI boundary; ordinary rejected credentials, cancellation, offline sync state and form validation must not produce non-fatals. Never include email, password, token, cloud/profile/server IDs, URLs or raw error messages.
- **Actual:** `CloudAccountViewModel` has no `Analytics` dependency and contains no Analytics or diagnostic calls. It catches exceptions for session restore, auth, account link, storage usage, sign-out, delete, sync and backup preference changes and updates UI state only. OAuth/coroutine cancellations are rethrown without an explicit bounded outcome. `feature/cloud-account/ui/build.gradle.kts` has no Analytics API dependency. The separate root navigation owner emits only the screen-view route event/breadcrumb.
- **Frequency:** Deterministic source absence across all reviewed Cloud Account operation boundaries; no runtime action has yet been performed in this continuation.
- **Evidence:** [Cloud Account instrumentation source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cloud-account-instrumentation-source-audit.txt); paths listed there. This is source evidence, not device/runtime coverage.
- **Root cause:** Feature-level telemetry was never wired into CloudAccountViewModel; typed cloud operation events and an explicit cancellation/failure reporting policy do not exist. Route exposure was added independently in Home navigation.
- **Affected files:** `feature/cloud-account/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/cloudaccount/ui/CloudAccountViewModel.kt`, `feature/cloud-account/ui/build.gradle.kts`, tests under `feature/cloud-account/ui/src/commonTest`, and a typed event model/test in `lib/analytics/api` (or equivalent existing hierarchy). Do not duplicate event emission in repositories.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `96e69ea50977a1da74415d748dfb2de83fcf981e` (`feat(cloud-account): instrument operation outcomes [QA-BUG-0082, test 778]`). Contract: [Cloud Account event dictionary](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cloud-account-event-dictionary.txt).
- **Retest:** Host PASS: CloudAccountOperationTelemetryTest 7/7 and AnalyticsParameterSanitizerTest 34/34; Android debug assembly succeeded. On the installed Samsung build from `9cd6ff56`, route exposure opened the signed-out Cloud Account screen; local DebugAnalyticsManager showed session-restore attempt/success and the Google action attempt plus correlated OAuth-start breadcrumb. OAuth remained in Browser at the debug loopback error page, so no terminal has yet been verified and the screen/action coverage is incomplete. The observed local output is not Firebase delivery. Credential-dependent actions and controlled failure recovery remain BLOCKED; Analytics ingestion is waived and Crashlytics delivery unverified.

## QA-BUG-0083 — Cloud profile-link conflict suppresses sign-out failure

- **Title:** Profile-link conflict cleanup ignores sign-out exceptions before rendering SignedOut.
- **Severity / user impact:** Medium, SUSPECTED; if cleanup fails before local cloud credentials/session are cleared, the screen can claim the account is signed out while an authenticated session remains, creating stale state and confusing recovery/privacy expectations. No Samsung failure reproduction has been performed.
- **Status:** SUSPECTED by source audit; runtime behavior and whether the repository's non-cancellable cleanup always prevents the mismatch are unverified. No fix implemented.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Cloud Account case 374; shared failure/recovery cases 742, 748–749; new Cloud Account coverage case 778.
- **Device/build/commit:** Source audit on the final Samsung candidate context: `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0, `BP4A.251205.006.S921BXXSGDZG1`; `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), last verified APK SHA-256 `d1f5202462a20294aa5a8c3fd227266deace01e661dfb37c3973fea0b0fafe06`, app source through `88f44a5f0d502419413dcf95c7c63ad30c2a651f`; documentation audit HEAD `155b3110919d473efe1fb429b4fd1df482a57115`. Source-only, no UI action/device failure injection.
- **Preconditions:** Signed-in account has a profile-link conflict; a controlled sign-out failure occurs while dismissing the link-profile confirmation or after a conflicting link result.
- **Exact reproduction (fixture not available):** Cause `signOutCloudAccountUseCase()` inside `signOutAfterLinkConflict()` to throw before session invalidation/local profile-session replacement completes. Inspect both the resulting Cloud Account view state and the actual observed auth/session state.
- **Expected:** Do not silently present a terminal SignedOut state until local session cleanup is verified. Surface a recoverable error, preserve accurate auth state, and emit one correlated unexpected-failure diagnostic/report without identifiers or credentials.
- **Actual (source-only suspicion):** `signOutAfterLinkConflict()` catches any non-cancellation exception and discards it, then unconditionally writes `CloudAuthState.SignedOut`, clears `profileLink`, sets `isLoading=false`, and displays the conflict error. The repository's sign-out implementation has a `finally` cleanup path, so whether that path rules out a persistent mismatch under all failures is unverified.
- **Frequency:** Not reproduced; one suspicious catch-and-overwrite path found in source.
- **Evidence:** [Cloud Account instrumentation/source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cloud-account-instrumentation-source-audit.txt), `CloudAccountViewModel.signOutAfterLinkConflict()` and `SupabaseCloudAccountDataRepository.signOut()`.
- **Root cause:** Suspected error suppression in conflict cleanup; operation result is not propagated to the UI state decision. Must verify the local session cleanup invariant before classifying as a confirmed functional bug.
- **Affected files:** `feature/cloud-account/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/cloudaccount/ui/CloudAccountViewModel.kt`; potentially cloud repository/domain if a failure boundary needs a typed result.
- **Fix reference / commit:** Pending investigation. Do not change behavior based on source suspicion alone; add a focused fault-injection test and verify intended fallback first.
- **Retest:** NOT RUN/BLOCKED until a synthetic conflicting-link fixture and safe local sign-out fault injection are available. Do not create/use cloud credentials or mutate retained app profiles to produce this state.

## QA-BUG-0084 — Cloud auth session can outlive pending-auth persistence failure

- **Title:** Successful Cloud sign-in/registration can leave its session active when the local pending-auth record fails to persist.
- **Severity / user impact:** High/recoverable, CONFIRMED by source path (runtime fixture not yet executed). The user sees an auth error after a server session was created; the auth-state observer can expose the signed-in account and profile-link dialog, but LinkCloudAccountUseCase requires the missing pending-auth record and cannot complete. The screen can become trapped in a misleading partial-auth state, and retry may behave as though the user is already signed in.
- **Status:** CONFIRMED by deterministic code path; rollback and cleanup implementation committed. Samsung write-failure reproduction unavailable.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Cloud Account cases 363–364, 739, new case 779; shared cases 742–743, 748–749.
- **Device/build/commit:** Required Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0, build `BP4A.251205.006.S921BXXSGDZG1`; package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), last verified APK SHA-256 `d1f5202462a20294aa5a8c3fd227266deace01e661dfb37c3973fea0b0fafe06`, app source through `88f44a5f0d502419413dcf95c7c63ad30c2a651f`; source-audit HEAD `4983f9d086dc44ecfea901738b41bab8bf21ebf9`. No cloud credentials or device mutation were used.
- **Preconditions:** A host fake returns a successful signed-in `CloudAccount`; `PendingCloudAuthenticationRepository.save()` throws before a usable record is persisted.
- **Exact reproduction:** `SignInCloudAccountUseCase.completeSignIn()` obtains a signed-in account, then calls `pendingAuthenticationRepository.save()`. Inject the local save exception. The account repository's auth session was already established; its auth-state Flow can update the ViewModel to SignedIn while loading. The UI's generic error path enables link confirmation, but LinkCloudAccountUseCase cannot find the expected pending-auth record. Registration has the same persistence boundary for signed-in results.
- **Expected:** Do not leave an authenticated cloud session/link prompt active when required local auth state did not persist. Roll back/clear the local session and pending record, keep loading terminal, provide retryable signed-out UI, and emit one bounded local-persistence failure outcome/diagnostic without email, password, token or account/profile ID.
- **Actual:** Before fix, both auth use cases propagated the pending-record write exception without rolling back the newly established session or clearing a possibly partial record. The ViewModel then rendered a generic failure while auth observation could already have published SignedIn.
- **Frequency:** Deterministic if the local pending-auth write fails after successful email/OAuth auth; not induced on the physical device. Host fault-injection test is required.
- **Evidence:** [Cloud Account persistence failure source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cloud-account-persistence-failure-source-audit.txt). The source-level order is confirmed; no runtime/device reproduction is claimed.
- **Root cause:** Pending-auth local persistence is a required post-authentication step but its failure path has no compensating session rollback or cleanup.
- **Affected files:** `feature/cloud-account/domain/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/cloudaccount/domain/usecase/{SignInCloudAccountUseCase.kt,RegisterCloudAccountUseCase.kt}`, shared pending-auth persistence helper, and `CloudAuthenticationUseCasesTest.kt`.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `995e1f066ef1f8ba881447392dab851472f9231c` (`fix(cloud-account): roll back failed auth persistence [QA-BUG-0084, test 779]`).
- **Retest:** Host PASS: `CloudAuthenticationUseCasesTest` 16/16 on iOS Simulator target, including sign-in/registration write failure, partial pending-record cleanup, sign-out rollback failure, and cancellation cleanup. Android debug assembly succeeded with this source. Physical-device failure injection is BLOCKED until an authorized disposable account and safe local-write-failure fixture exist; do not use the retained profile or submit credentials. No Samsung failure result claimed. Firebase ingestion is waived; Crashlytics delivery remains separately unverified.

## QA-BUG-0085 — Google OAuth cancellation is classified as authentication failure

- **Title:** Returning from Google OAuth without authorization is surfaced and measured as generic authentication failure rather than cancellation.
- **Severity / user impact:** Medium, CONFIRMED by deterministic source path. Before the fix, users who intentionally cancelled OAuth saw generic error state instead of a clear return to the sign-in form, and operation reporting could not distinguish cancellation from rejected credentials. Runtime behavior still requires a valid provider page; no account state should be created.
- **Status:** Fix implemented and PASS for host-level typed callback and Samsung app-side external-activity/system-Back cancellation paths. Provider-page callback/runtime retest remains BLOCKED by QA-BUG-0086; no credentials were submitted.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Cloud Account cases 367, 731, 778; shared cases 742, 748–749.
- **Device/build/commit:** Required Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0, build `BP4A.251205.006.S921BXXSGDZG1`; package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), debug APK SHA-256 `e30ad041567e2e043bed08e5fc6662634abd86c3a13b717a057373212e8a3c3e`, source commit `9cd6ff56ee48f8297e0cd2ebc095a3313d205f3d`.
- **Preconditions:** Signed-out Cloud Account screen; begin Google OAuth, then use browser cancellation/system Back or deliver a cancellation callback without an authorization code.
- **Exact reproduction (source path):** `CloudOAuthCallbackRegistry.handleRedirect()` completes its deferred exceptionally with a private `CloudOAuthException` (a normal `Exception`) when there is no callback code. `SupabaseCloudAccountDataRepository.signInWithGoogle()` propagates it; `CloudAccountOperationTelemetry.execute()` only maps `CancellationException` to `cloud_account_operation_cancelled`, so the exception follows `cloud_account_operation_failed` with `authentication_failed`. `CloudAccountViewModel.signInWithGoogle()` then invokes generic `showError`, presenting the generic error state. The failure predicate does not send this exception as a Crashlytics non-fatal, but the user cancellation is misclassified.
- **Expected:** An OAuth denial/cancel returns to a usable signed-out form with loading stopped, no partial account/session, exactly one bounded cancelled event and correlated cancelled breadcrumb, and no Crashlytics exception. Actual provider/authentication failures remain failed outcomes.
- **Actual before fix:** Source mapped missing-code callbacks, including OAuth cancellation, to a generic exception; UI reported generic error and operation Analytics labeled it authentication failure. The fix now produces typed cancellation, a bounded cancelled event/breadcrumb, and resets the form to SignedOut without a non-fatal.
- **Frequency:** Deterministic for a callback accepted without an authorization code; not yet executed on Samsung.
- **Evidence:** [Cloud Account OAuth cancellation source audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cloud-account-oauth-cancellation-source-audit.txt). Source evidence only; no device/runtime PASS claimed.
- **Root cause:** Callback registry discards the OAuth error category into an untyped exception, while telemetry recognizes only coroutine cancellation.
- **Affected files:** `lib/cloud/implementation/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/cloud/implementation/CloudOAuthCallbackRegistry.kt`, `feature/cloud-account/data/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/cloudaccount/data/SupabaseCloudAccountDataRepository.kt`, domain OAuth error mapping if needed, and Cloud Account operation tests.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `9cd6ff56ee48f8297e0cd2ebc095a3313d205f3d` (`fix(cloud-account): classify OAuth cancellation [QA-BUG-0085, test 367]`), separate from QA-BUG-0084 and QA-BUG-0082.
- **Retest:** Host PASS: OAuth callback registry 3/3, including typed `access_denied`, provider-failure separation, sanitized message and app cancellation; Cloud Account telemetry 7/7, including typed cancellation without non-fatal; Android debug assembly succeeded. Samsung app-side PASS: immediate system Back from the external browser returned to a clean signed-out form, emitted one cancelled terminal (`oauth_cancelled`) and correlated start/stage/terminal breadcrumbs, with no non-fatal. A separate long-open attempt terminated as `oauth_timed_out` and was not counted as cancellation. Actual provider-page cancellation remains BLOCKED because the installed debug endpoint is loopback (QA-BUG-0086). [Samsung route/cancel evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cloud-account-oauth-back-cancel-retest.txt). Firebase ingestion is waived; Crashlytics delivery separately unverified.

## QA-BUG-0086 — Samsung debug build targets Google OAuth at device loopback

- **Title:** Google sign-in from the Samsung debug build opens an unreachable loopback URL instead of a provider authorization page.
- **Severity / user impact:** Medium for physical-device debug QA; confirmed reachability blocker in this tested debug setup. Google sign-in cannot proceed on the physical phone, so users cannot reach the provider page to complete or cancel authentication normally. No account state is changed by the observed attempt. This is not established as a release/production product defect.
- **Status:** CONFIRMED runtime blocker for the installed debug build; product-source defect not established. No app configuration change made. Release/production behavior is not inferred.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Cloud Account cases 366–367, 731, 778.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0, build `BP4A.251205.006.S921BXXSGDZG1`; package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), debug APK SHA-256 `e30ad041567e2e043bed08e5fc6662634abd86c3a13b717a057373212e8a3c3e`, source commit `9cd6ff56ee48f8297e0cd2ebc095a3313d205f3d`. Installed with `adb install -r`; app data was preserved.
- **Preconditions:** Installed debug variant, signed-out Cloud Account screen, device is connected; do not enter cloud credentials or authenticate.
- **Exact reproduction:** Open Settings → Sync & Backup; tap “Continue with Google.” Samsung Browser launches to host `127.0.0.1` and displays “This site can't be reached” / “null is unreachable.” No form credentials were entered and no OAuth authorization was completed.
- **Expected:** Launch a reachable configured provider authorization page; the user can then authorize or cancel without credentials being required for a cancellation test.
- **Actual:** The debug merged manifest supplies a loopback Supabase URL. On the Samsung, loopback resolves to the phone itself; Browser shows an unreachable page instead of an OAuth flow.
- **Frequency:** Reproduced once on this installed debug build; configuration/source audit confirms the debug metadata host is loopback. No claim about the release manifest/runtime.
- **Evidence:** [Sanitized endpoint/config audit](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cloud-account-google-oauth-debug-endpoint.txt); screenshot [OAuth launch](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cloud-account-oauth-start.png). The source audit records only the host and presence/length of key material, never the key value or full URL.
- **Root cause:** `androidApp/src/debug/AndroidManifest.xml` configures the local Supabase endpoint on loopback, but the Samsung has no active `adb reverse` mapping and the host's local auth health endpoint timed out during this run. The debug fixture is not currently reachable from the phone.
- **Affected files:** Debug cloud endpoint configuration in `androidApp/src/debug/AndroidManifest.xml` and its merged debug manifest. No credentials/config values have been copied into QA evidence.
- **Fix reference / commit:** No source fix. A valid local Supabase host/ADB reverse fixture or authorized reachable QA endpoint is needed; the health probe timed out and `adb reverse --list` was empty. Do not substitute the production endpoint or change cloud configuration without authorization.
- **Retest:** Provider-page success/cancellation remains BLOCKED until a reachable, authorized QA endpoint is available. Immediate Samsung Browser Back separately returned to Parrot and passed the app-side cancellation path; this does not pass Google provider-flow coverage or fix endpoint reachability. Firebase Analytics ingestion is waived; DebugAnalyticsManager evidence is local-only.

## QA-BUG-0087 — Create-account mode retains sign-in helper copy

- **Title:** Switching the Cloud Account form to Create account leaves helper text that instructs the user to sign in and create an account below.
- **Severity / user impact:** Low, confirmed visual/copy inconsistency; the form controls and disabled state correctly switch to account creation, but the contradictory helper can mislead a new user about the current mode and next action.
- **Status:** FIXED and Samsung-retested PASS. The signed-out form now uses mode-specific helper copy; mode transitions and submit/consent state remain usable.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Cloud Account cases 359 and 732.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0, build `BP4A.251205.006.S921BXXSGDZG1`; package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), APK SHA-256 `e30ad041567e2e043bed08e5fc6662634abd86c3a13b717a057373212e8a3c3e`, source commit `9cd6ff56ee48f8297e0cd2ebc095a3313d205f3d`.
- **Preconditions:** Signed-out Cloud Account screen in Sign in mode; empty email/password; terms consent unchecked.
- **Exact reproduction:** Settings → Sync & Backup; tap “New to Parrot Cloud? Create an account.” The form changes to “Create account,” reveals the terms checkbox, and disables submission; inspect the helper directly below the primary button.
- **Expected:** Helper text matches Create account mode, explains the form's current action, and does not tell the user to sign in or to switch modes again.
- **Actual:** The helper remains “Enter your email and password, then sign in. New here? Create an account below.” while the Create account form is already active.
- **Frequency:** Reproduced once on the installed Samsung build; one mode transition observed.
- **Evidence:** [Pre-fix create-account screenshot](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cloud-account-create-mode.png); local mode event at 06:54:34 CEST records `mode=create_account`.
- **Root cause:** `AccountFormContent` rendered `cloud_account_sign_in_hint` for every disabled form regardless of `CloudAccountMode`; therefore the Create account form inherited sign-in-only helper copy.
- **Affected files:** `feature/cloud-account/ui/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/retro99/cloudaccount/ui/CloudAccountScreen.kt`; `translations/src/commonMain/composeResources/values/strings.xml`.
- **Fix reference / commit:** `f876cba6720b3e5c6ac63c079c615bd6d40e0d45` (`fix(cloud-account): show mode-specific helper [QA-BUG-0087, test 359]`). The UI selects a dedicated create-account hint that reminds the user to accept the terms; Sign in retains its existing hint.
- **Retest:** PASS on the physical Samsung after `adb install -r` with app data preserved. Debug APK SHA-256 `3895ecb2b51673e7c92b69f8ccc2e7831a8d6dfb7e5d3f1a2c5ed3037ab990df`; package 0.4.5 (21), PID `11324`, install time 07:01:35 CEST. Cloud Account UI host tests 7/7 passed and `:androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeded. At 07:03:09 Create account showed “Enter your email and password, then accept the terms to create your account.” and a disabled button with unchecked consent; at 07:03:18 Sign in restored the original sign-in helper; at 07:03:27 Create account again restored its matching helper. Exactly three local mode events matched three transitions. No credentials were entered. [Samsung retest evidence](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cloud-account-cases-359-mode-copy-retest.txt). Firebase ingestion is waived; Crashlytics was not applicable to this expected UI path.

## QA-BUG-0088 — Cloud Account email validation has no inline result or telemetry

- **Title:** Malformed Cloud Account email input receives no field-level feedback, and validation failure/recovery is invisible to Analytics and diagnostics.
- **Severity / user impact:** Medium, confirmed degraded/recoverable behavior. The Sign in/Create account action remains disabled for malformed input, but users are not told which field is invalid or how to correct it; validation and recovery outcomes cannot be measured or traced.
- **Status:** CONFIRMED on the Samsung debug build; pre-fix evidence and event contract recorded before source edits. Fix/retest pending.
- **Screen/test IDs:** Cloud Account cases 360 and 778.
- **Device/build/commit:** Samsung `RFCWC0SSVDM`, SM-S921B, Android 16/API 36, One UI 8.0 (`ro.build.version.oneui=80500`), build `BP4A.251205.006.S921BXXSGDZG1`, gesture navigation; package `com.retro99.parrot` 0.4.5 (21), debug APK SHA-256 `3895ecb2b51673e7c92b69f8ccc2e7831a8d6dfb7e5d3f1a2c5ed3037ab990df`, built from `f876cba6720b3e5c6ac63c079c615bd6d40e0d45`; device PID `11324`. Repository HEAD during this pre-fix documentation is `1b23c89c`; installed code was the `f876cba6` build.
- **Preconditions:** Original Default profile and unauthenticated server/book fixture unchanged; signed-out Cloud Account screen, Create account and Sign in modes. Only synthetic, local-only email/password strings; never submit.
- **Exact reproduction:** Settings → Sync & Backup. Enter malformed synthetic email values (one without an `@`/domain and one incomplete `qa@`), observe the field and form; then replace with a syntactically valid reserved-example address and observe. Do not submit. Clear both fields.
- **Expected:** Non-empty malformed email shows an accessible, localized inline field error before submission; correcting it removes the error. Emit one bounded validation-failed outcome and diagnostic breadcrumb per invalid episode, and a correlated recovery outcome/breadcrumb when corrected. Do not send a Crashlytics non-fatal for ordinary validation. Never include field contents.
- **Actual:** Neither malformed value produced error text or error styling on the email field; only the pre-existing generic form helper/disabled submit communicated anything. A valid-format value produced no explicit validation-success/recovery signal. Logcat contained the screen/restore route events but no validation Analytics event or diagnostic breadcrumb.
- **Frequency:** Reproduced with two malformed values in the same form session; valid syntax was subsequently entered. No request was submitted and no auth result was induced.
- **Evidence:** [Sanitized pre-fix run and screenshots](manual-qa-evidence/2026-09-29/cloud-account-case-360-email-validation-pre-fix.txt). Screenshots show only synthetic `qa@` / reserved `example.com` test values. Password was synthetic, cleared, and not submitted; the OS Bitwarden “Save password?” prompt was declined with “No, thanks.”
- **Root cause:** `CloudAccountViewModel.updateFormState()` only reduced email validity to `isSubmitEnabled`; `AccountFormContent` had no email error state/supporting text. There was no typed email-validation Analytics event or validation breadcrumb.
- **Affected files:** Cloud Account ViewModel/state/screen; Cloud Account Analytics event API and UI tests; English string resources; event dictionary and QA docs.
- **Fix reference / commit:** Pending. The UI state and one-per-transition validation telemetry are inherently coupled: the same non-empty invalid→valid state transition drives the inline message, bounded result event and correlated diagnostic breadcrumb. Keep ordinary validation out of Crashlytics and avoid per-keystroke/payload logging.
- **Retest:** NOT RUN. Do not proceed to case 361 until the fix is built, installed via `adb install -r` with data preserved, and this case plus validation recovery are verified on the Samsung. Firebase Analytics ingestion remains waived; local events alone are not delivery proof.
